See `feedback-too-many-words.md`, `feedback-confirm-before-writing-lore.md`, `feedback-draft-only-when-user-triggers.md`, `feedback-schemas-as-enforcement-overreach.md`, `feedback-layered-decomposition-for-open-ended-asks.md`, `feedback-mvp-minimalism.md`, `feedback-comply-promptly-after-repeated-pushback.md`.\n\n## Key Constraints\n\n- `lore-context.md` ≤ 50K tokens; **shape over size** — working-knowledge + summary-topic references, not an index (see `lore-context-shape-discipline.md`).\n- Lore topics: atomic, <5K tokens preferred, plain markdown, no frontmatter.\n- Descriptor frontmatter: `lore-repo.md` = `description` + `version` (+ optional `repos:`); `role.md` = `description` only.\n- Command filenames: lowercase/digits/hyphens, ≤64 chars.\n- Placeholders: `<workspace>`, `<lore-agent-repo>`, `<guest-lore-agent-repo>`, `<agent-name>`, `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`.\n- **CWD safety:** never `cd` when later tools depend on cwd — use `git -C <repo>`. **Portable shell:** assume BSD/macOS, no GNU-only binaries (`timeout`); bound commands via the Bash-tool timeout.\n- See `conventions.md`, `placeholder-vocabulary.md`, `tooling-cwd-safety.md`, `portable-shell-in-framework-docs.md`.\n\n## Onboarding-Doc Authoring\n\nCo-authoring framework onboarding docs for adopting teams is part of the role. Two distinct genres now exist: **`onboarding-doc-narrative-pattern.md`** (long-form prose pitching a human reader) and **`paste-link-installer-doc-genre.md`** (a doc written *to the AI agent* as the literal installer, meant to be pasted as a link — shipped as `QUICKSTART.md` + per-engine `INSTALL-<ENGINE>.md`). Load the identity-layer framings first, then the toolkit: the two genre topics above, `use-cases-via-parallel-consult-pattern.md`, `terminology-domain-collision-trap.md`, `agent-as-universal-working-environment.md`, `in-flight-skill-teaching-pattern.md`. Pre-ship review for either genre uses `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`'s multi-lens fan-out; the installer genre additionally needs the **AI-installer (literal executor)** lens (`ai-installer-review-lens.md`) — it catches execution-fidelity bugs (e.g. `skill-doc-filename-divergence-bug-class.md`) the newcomer/editorial lenses miss. Landing-page placement of a self-referential/meta example differs from long-narrative placement — primacy goes to the strongest CTA; see `onboarding-doc-narrative-pattern.md` § placement note. A recurring funnel bug: an author writing from the fresh-start perspective railroads readers into create-your-first-agent and leaves the **team-join path** invisible at every layer (README prose, QUICKSTART, and the INSTALL AI-agent preambles) — check all layers, and keep the fork question verbatim-identical across sites (`onboarding-funnel-team-join-path.md`). Adopter-facing prose carries the product name **\"Lore Agents\"** while the engine keeps `lore-framework`/`lr` (`lore-agents-product-name.md`). First instance: the Activities team's intro doc.\n\n## Active Design Explorations\n\n- **lr-dev / Dark Factory (DF)** — major direction; a `lr` module for SDLC automation toward an autonomous \"dark factory\" SDLC. Per-repo artifacts + narrative context live in a `<repo>-df` backbone (a `repo-lore/<file>/` mirror: `file-lore.md` narrative landing + flat structured aspect subdirs like `ula/`). Skills not agents; persistence external. First aspect: **AIQA/ULA** (`/lr:df-repo-init`, `/lr:df-ula-file`) — unit-level analysis with a bug-verification track, BETA. The DF/ULA design thread is closed and the module ships as BETA. Anchor: `lr-dev-direction.md`; see `df-per-repo-backbone.md`, `aiqa-ula-feature.md`, `df-module-conventions.md`, `workflow-primitive-operational-notes.md`.\n- **Autonomous agents / Lore Beings** — agents as always-on background collaborators with persistent task state, raising for input only when needed. Concrete steps taken: `/lr:spawn-teammate` (multi-agent substrate, v10) and the v18 **`lr-wait`** primitive — the first *inbound-signal* step: an agent blocks on an event and an external actor (cron/CI/webhook/human, via `lr-emit`) wakes it with text. **The beings design is settled (2026-07-19): the module is _Lore Beings_.** A being is an ordinary lore agent plus a `being.md` descriptor; the **Being Keeper** (`lrb`) is deterministic substrate (never an LLM). **MVP is CLI-only**; engines are explicit user config. Budget = daily-USD spawn gate + per-task wall-clock kill. **Engine kinds: `claude`, `codex`, and `cursor`** — cursor landed in framework v28: requires `--plugin-dir` at `engines add`, claude-shaped JSON result + flat-cost fallback. **Keeper-specific real-engine lifecycle coverage now exists** (`tests/lifecycle/keeper_harness.py` + `test_lrb_lifecycle.py`, 10 scenarios after the 2026-07-20 fifth pass added B2/B3, separate higher-blast-radius gate `LR_LIFECYCLE_KEEPER=1`, verified claude 6/6 + codex 1/1 + cursor 1/1 at the recommended-minimum tier). That fifth pass found and fixed a real production bug, not just a coverage gap: `cursor-agent`'s sandboxed shell tool escapes `_kill`'s `killpg` by running spawned commands in a freshly `setsid`'d session, which left a real orphaned process on the test machine before the fix — `_kill` now also walks the full ppid-descendant tree and signals every descendant directly, enumerated *before* any ancestor is signaled (killing the ancestor first risks the OS reparenting a survivor to PID 1 and erasing the ppid link). **The Keeper substrate shipped BETA in v28, and v29 added the in-engine command surface `/lr:being`** — one skill with subcommands (status/init/create/validate/logs/keeper/engine/workspace/pause/resume) over the same deterministic `lrb` CLI, rather than a `lrb-*` skill per operation. Ship record: v28 at framework commit `5e00209`, tag `lr--v1.28.0`; the standard lifecycle gate passed by persisted broad matrix plus targeted reruns. Real-engine verification sharpened two per-kind contract gaps (both backlog schema decisions, not silently patched): the `cursor` kind is empirically cost-blind (no `total_cost_usd`), so its flat `--session-cost-usd` fallback is load-bearing not optional; the `claude` kind has no `--plugin-dir` field, so a claude-kind being needs a wrapper-script `command` to load `lr:` skills at all. Two further findings from that same review pass — budget-enforcement edge cases and an unattended-full-permission trust gap — were deliberately deferred to the backlog rather than fixed. Chronicler week-long soak and persistent `--launchd` install still user-triggered. Open gap: headless permissions (`permission_mode: full` vs future scoped-tools), and self-scheduling under the safe default. Anchor: `lore-beings-design.md`; see `cursor-agent-real-invocation-contract.md`, `engine-kinds-design-decision.md`, `lifecycle-testing-harness.md` § Keeper coverage, `keeper-spawn-prompt-boilerplate-distraction.md`, `lore-beings-mvp-takeover-review.md`, `kill-tree-enumerate-before-signal-ordering.md`, `codex-exec-real-invocation-contract.md`, `macos-ps-o-multi-field-single-line.md`, `agent-being-consciousness-substrate-split.md`, `unenforceable-caps-are-prompt-theater.md`, `feedback-mvp-minimalism.md`, `autonomous-agents-vision.md`, `wait-primitive-feature.md`, `framework-improvements-backlog.md` § Major Directions § Autonomous Agents / Lore Beings.\n- **Multi-engine portability (Codex, Cursor)** — **the ports are shipped, not in flight.** Claude\n  Code, Codex, and Cursor are all Tier-1 supported, so a mixed-engine team shares one agent repo.\n  Claude Code remains the reference path: shared procedure docs are written in Claude terms, and other\n  engines override only at binding points. What made this tractable: the knowledge substrate (agent\n  repos, `lore-repo.md`, `role.md`, `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, git) was already engine-agnostic, so\n  the port was **packaging, not redesign** — the whole surface is **5 adapter bindings** via the\n  `docs/engines/` convention (framework-root, invocation-syntax, subagent-spawn, memory-file,\n  runtime-bounding) plus Boot Step-0 engine selection. Both engines have native in-session subagents\n  (Codex `spawn_agent`/`wait_agent`; Cursor `Task`), so the feared Tier-B nucleus is proven, and one\n  repo carries both skill namespaces (canonical `skills/<skill>/` for Claude, `.cursor-skills/lr-<skill>/`\n  wrappers for Cursor, kept in sync by `scripts/sync-cursor-skills` and `/lr:check` #21).\n\n  Standing operational facts worth carrying: **trust rollout/tool-call logs, not model self-report**,\n  when validating an engine path; Codex's default sandbox blocks `.git` writes and network, so the\n  supported finalization path needs `.git` writable through launch/config (a commit-blocked run is\n  degraded fallback, not a merge failure); Codex per-agent shortcut register/unregister/list remains an\n  unvalidated implementation gap. **Separate sessions on separate engines coordinating on a real task**\n  (not `/lr:attach`, which is single-executor multi-agent) now has a validated substrate: a shared\n  append-only folder (worked example: `workdir/v31-feedback/`), reusable across message-vocabulary\n  changes — see `cross-engine-team-substrate-validated.md`. Two rules fell out of running it for real:\n  **check for same agent identity before any session writes** (two engines can be the same booted\n  agent against the same repo — designate one write-owner, `same-agent-multiple-engines-single-writer.md`)\n  and **don't relay a user decision from one session into the shared channel as settled authority for\n  another session** (`cross-engine-relay-not-attributable-authority.md`). A genuinely different engine\n  also catches design flaws same-engine self-review (even multi-lens) misses — a distinct axis from the\n  sandboxed-review/self-report family, worth the coordination cost on high-stakes design decisions,\n  not routine changes (`independent-engine-review-catches-structural-blind-spots.md`). The **\"framework\n  is prose executed by the model\"** risk is\n  empirically retired for the exercised paths, and the substrate half has quantitative backing — the\n  quality benchmark showed positive lore-utilization uplift on every engine+model config, with the\n  nuance that **model–engine fit beats model tier**. Cross-engine support is a *supporting* fact in\n  positioning, not the headline (the 2026-07-20 landscape re-survey found competitors claiming it too);\n  lead with the triad. Anchor: `multi-engine-portability-direction.md`; per-engine entry points\n  `claude-engine-capabilities.md`, `codex-engine-capabilities.md`, `cursor-engine-capabilities.md`;\n  see also `docs-engines-convention.md`, `subagent-as-optimization-vs-subagent-as-semantics.md`,\n  `claude-coupling-inventory-and-port-tiers.md`, `cursor-dual-skill-tree-one-repo.md`,\n  `quality-benchmark-feature.md`, `positioning-triad-differentiation.md`, `port-landing-next-steps.md`.\n- **Lore housekeeping / consolidation \"sleep\" pass** and the **simplification/subtraction** review item — active follow-ups from the 2026-06-13 architecture review; see `framework-improvements-backlog.md`. That review's settled dispositions (incl. DF-inside-`lr` and team-shared/multi-author as deliberate, not defects — don't re-raise) live in `architecture-review-dispositions.md`. A newer 2026-07-02 review added two further backlog items (post-merge diff verification, recall-time staleness surfacing) — see `framework-improvements-backlog.md` § Merge Quality, § Search / Scaling.\n- Parked: workdir-as-reference-library; vector-DB search (until >100 topics/agent); the session-as-durable-artifact cluster (boot auto-push, boot-context cache, suspend/resume, JSONL archive). All in `framework-improvements-backlog.md`.\n- **v25 workspace layer (pull + init)** — implemented locally in `lore-framework` commit `0311ab6`.\n  Hard renames: workspace-sync→workspace-pull, init→workspace-init. Two-level repo declarations\n  (`lore-workspace.md` + domain `repos:`), optional workspace-as-git-repo envelope, cursor wrapper\n  regeneration, checks #22–23. Full lifecycle remains the pre-push gate. See\n  `v25-workspace-pull-init-design.md`, `workspace-meta-repo-pattern.md`.\n\n## Current State\n\nWorkspace holds three canonical repos: **`lore-framework/`** (plugin), **`lore-framework-dev/`**\n(this repo — lore-architect lore, tests, drafts), and **`lore-agents/`** (personal agents).\n\n`lore-framework/` is **clean and pushed at v31** on `main`. Generated per-agent shortcuts no longer bake an\nabsolute, version-pinned path to `agent-boot.md`; they self-locate framework authority off the\nsession's actively installed boot skill instead, fixing a defect where a shortcut pinned to a dead\nplugin-cache path after an upgrade. Designed via the three-engine (Claude/Codex/Cursor) shared-folder\nreview recorded at `agents/lore-architect/workdir/v31-feedback/`. If the subagent's *independence from the caller* is the deliverable\n(`/lr:trilens-loop`), serialization destroys the feature — the procedure must stop and report. Profile\ndegradation clauses therefore need carve-outs, not blanket rules, and \"the conservative profile passed\nthe whole suite\" only ever meant it was adequate for the procedures that existed then. Codex\nnative packaging is resolved in v25: legacy Claude marketplace fallback still works, native\n`.agents/plugins/marketplace.json` is preferred when present, and `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` is the\nCodex version-bearing manifest. Public submission also needs reviewer-facing metadata (`MARKETPLACE.md`\ndirectory copy + root `PRIVACY.md`) and precise separation between runtime release identity,\nsubmission-support files, and per-engine verified publication status. See `engine-marketplace-readiness.md`, `plugin-distribution.md`,\n`cursor-plugin-distribution-update-model.md`, `plugin-manifest-versioning.md`.\n\nPositioning copy for README/marketplace submission must lead with the **triad** (named\nrole-based agents + deliberate reflect/merge curation + cross-agent collaboration), not\ncross-engine support — see `positioning-triad-differentiation.md`. **Re-survey the\ncompetitive landscape (`similar-projects-landscape.md`) before any positioning-sensitive\nship** — README rewrite, marketplace submission, public announcement — the space moved\nmaterially in just 18 days as of the 2026-07-20 re-survey.\n\n## Boot & Freshness\n\nBoot (`agent-boot.md`, single source of truth): discover agent → auto-pull repo → version check → read `role.md` + `lore-context.md` → detect teammate spawn → confirm. **Boot loads only those two files; topics are read on demand.** Repos auto-pull at every session-context boundary (boot, attach, pre-merge) to match the team's latest pushed state; `/lr:pull-lore` is the manual refresh. See `freshness-contracts-at-session-boundaries.md`, `auto-pull-mechanism.md`.\n\n`version-check.md`'s nested-repo guard has a macOS-specific trap: \"resolve both to real paths\" is not\nself-executing prose — a weak model filled the gap with bare `pwd`, which disagrees with git's\n`--show-toplevel` under macOS's `/var`→`/private/var` symlink, producing a false \"not its own git root\"\nverdict. Fixed by naming the exact `os.path.realpath()` one-liner instead of the vague instruction. See\n`macos-var-symlink-realpath-ambiguity.md`.\n\n## Cross-Agent Collaboration\n\n- **`/lr:recall [hint]`** — search lore of already-loaded agents (host + guests); fan-out per agent.\n- **`/lr:consult <agent> [hint]`** — one-shot question to an unloaded agent; a subagent boots it, answers with file pointers, exits.\n- **`/lr:attach <agent>`** — load another agent as a sustained guest; host stays sole executor, host-wins on conflicts.\n- **`/lr:spawn-teammate` (BETA)** — spawn agents as Agent Teams teammates for parallel panes; the teammate's primary interlocutor is the user, not the lead.\n\nSee `lore-search-pattern.md`, `consult-pattern.md`, `attach-pattern.md`, `spawn-teammate-feature.md`, `teammate-conventions.md`.\n\n## Session Takeover (BETA)\n\n**`/lr:takeover`** converts engine-native session logs into a markdown digest so a new session on any engine can continue interrupted work. Codex, Claude Code, and Cursor are supported (`scripts/session-takeover` — list, convert, render). Cursor pairs tool results from `store.db` to JSONL `tool_use` batches via batch-window name matching; same-name parallel batches and interrupted sessions set `pairing_uncertain`. See `takeover-feature.md`, `cursor-takeover-batch-pairing.md`, `engine-session-log-formats.md`.\n\n## Finalization\n\nUser-triggered, four phases (`/lr:finalize` runs all; phases also run standalone): **reflect** (inline, host-first, per agent — needs session context) → **merge** (parallel subagents, one per agent booted as itself, file-driven — integrates reflections into `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, `role.md`) → **summarize** (host writes the canonical session summary + short guest summaries) → **commit+push** (one commit per touched repo; conflict-resolution on push rejection). Do not finalize unless the user triggers it. See `finalization-process.md`, `finalize.md`, `merge-in-booted-subagents.md`, `reflect-merge-execution-asymmetry.md`.\n\n## Versioning & Migration\n\n`lore-framework/VERSION` is the single source of truth; **the current shipped-and-pushed version is\nv31** (`lr-core` substrate, shipped under a recorded gate waiver — see Current State below,\n`v31-lr-core-parked-2026-07-25.md`). **v32** (version-agnostic registered boot shortcuts) is locally\ncommitted in both `lore-framework` and `lore-framework-dev` but **not yet merged to `main` or\npushed** — see Current State below and `versioning-release-types.md`'s v32 entry. Each\nagent repo stamps that version in its `lore-repo.md`, and four version-bearing plugin manifests mirror\n`1.<VERSION>.0` (`/lr:check` #19 enforces). A version is either **migration**, **release-notes-only**,\nor both, and independently **cache-affecting** or not — those two axes are orthogonal, and every ship\nrecords both in `versioning-release-types.md`, which holds the full per-version history. Read that\ntopic for what any given version contained; don't reconstruct it from here.\n\nShip mechanics that bite: verify `git HEAD` rather than trusting lore's \"commit pending\" (it\naccumulates across versions), and scan the whole history tail for gaps at each ship rather than only\nappending the current entry. See `versioning-release-types.md`, `plugin-manifest-versioning.md`,\n`cache-clear-footer-convention.md`, `update-process.md`, `release-commit-hash-from-tag.md`.\n\n## Consistency & Diagnostics\n\n- **`/lr:check`** — 23 content-consistency checks (descriptor/version, structure, references, size/state, drift, four-manifest #19, migration write-paths #20, cursor-tree parity #21, workspace-layer checks #22–23). At scale, prefer a deterministic script-based sweep for the mechanical subset (existence/version/glob checks) over an LLM read-through — checks #9–10 alone missed 14 dangling references in a 147-topic graph. See `consistency-checks.md`.\n- **`/lr:doctor`** — diagnoses runtime/environmental issues that escape content checks (esp. stale plugin cache) via an accreting ailment catalog. See `ailment-catalog-pattern.md`.\n\n## Operating Disciplines\n\nHow I work, especially at version ships and high-stakes lore edits:\n- **On VERSION bumps:** backfill `versioning-release-types.md` history, add the cache-clear footer if cache-affecting, bump all four version-bearing plugin manifests to `1.<VERSION>.0`, promote any newly-named principle to its own topic. (Full curation disciplines live in `role.md`.)\n- **Pre-ship review:** multi-lens review iterated until a round finds nothing worth fixing (convergence is the ship signal) — **run it via `/lr:trilens-loop`** rather than hand-assembling the fan-out; the skill enforces cold-context reviewer independence, the APPLIED/DECLINED ledger, the \"a silent round is not a clean round\" guard, and rail-removal disclosure, while lens *choice* and triage judgement stay mine. Brief reviewers with the **goal, not the rationale** — rationale pre-empts the criticism you're paying for. Two triage facts from measuring a live round: **~94% of a loop's tokens stay inside the subagents** (a reviewer's budget is its own reasoning, not the material handed to it — so withholding the diff is cost-neutral, and the exchange contract must be justified on independence and host-context preservation, never on token savings), and **convergent findings from two independent lenses are strong evidence, not redundancy** — reword rather than defend. Sonnet boot-as-self review remains the separate single-lens, role-as-perspective tool for high-stakes single edits. See `trilens-loop-feature.md`, `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`, `sonnet-subagent-review-pattern.md`. **Second, empirical leg (v18+):** for procedure docs covered by the lifecycle testing harness, also run the relevant scenarios against real engine execution before shipping — review catches reasoning issues, the harness catches model-execution-fidelity issues invisible to a strong-model reviewer — and the fidelity axis is **engine, not just model tier** (run scenarios on every engine, using the cheapest practical default tier unless explicitly overriding: Claude Code -> haiku, Codex -> gpt-5.4-mini, Cursor -> composer-2.5; the same model tier can behave differently by engine, and mid-procedure step insertions are the highest-risk for a silent skip). See `lifecycle-testing-harness.md`, `execution-testing-catches-blind-ambiguity.md`, `haiku-ambiguity-detector.md`.\n- **A gate result belongs to an artifact state** — a converged review loop and a green lifecycle run each certify only the tree they actually ran against. An edit landed after the gates pass is ungated: re-run the affected gate, or revert and file a follow-up. Never report \"converged and green\" for a tree neither gate saw, and record *which* state a result belongs to. Also: an environment failure mid-run (e.g. macOS TCC revocation) or the engine resolving a *different* plugin tree makes results **uninterpretable**, not red — fix and re-run rather than debugging the code under test. See `post-convergence-edits-need-their-own-gate.md`, `macos-documents-permission-loss-mid-session.md`.\n- **A gate cannot be a model self-report** — a gate must not be implemented in the medium it gates. Ask what evidence it rests on and whether the thing under test could have produced that evidence; if yes, it is a self-report wearing a gate's name. \"Both engines have coverage\" is not evidence parity. See `a-gate-cannot-be-a-model-self-report.md`.\n- **A failure list is a hypothesis until someone reads the transcripts** — an assertion message names what was observed, never why. Re-triage from stored logs before planning fixes; stored artifacts plus `stat` routinely beat fresh engine probes on cost and certainty. Classify each text assertion as mid-run (needs the transcript) or end-state (final message suffices) — mixing them changes what a test means per engine. See `v31-lifecycle-rerun-partial-green-2026-07-27.md`, `transcript-vs-final-message-assertions.md`.\n- **Decide where the guardrail lives before writing the topic** — lore is retrieved when a task cues it, and a one-off command cues nothing; a trap recorded only as knowledge protects nobody, including its author. Name the point-of-use site (script check, exact command in the doc, test) as part of the fix, and prefer a deterministic harness check over a human prep step. See `point-of-use-guardrails-beat-recorded-lore.md`, `docs-engines-convention.md` § Engine traps belong in the binding.\n- **Verify before asserting** — check filesystem/state directly before \"fixing\" a suspected bug; verify *which* bug, not just whether. **Pointed inward:** before declaring a known finding moot — especially a ship-blocking one — read the lore rule it rests on rather than reconstructing it; reconstruction keeps a rule's motivating case and drops its actual obligation, and those diverge precisely when dismissal feels justified (`check-own-lore-before-dismissing-a-finding.md`). Same reflex, two more sites: fetch volatile external facts (prices, model IDs, rate limits) live with a dated citation rather than trusting memory — \"couldn't verify\" licenses marking a value unavailable, not guessing; and after any scoped/read-only subagent or fork returns, verify its actual filesystem footprint (`git status`, `git worktree list`) rather than trusting its summary — a capable fork acts on the largest goal it can see in inherited context unless scoped *against* it explicitly. See `verify-before-acting-on-suspected-bugs.md`, `fetch-volatile-facts-live-not-memory.md`, `fork-scope-creep-under-standing-goal.md`.\n- **Curation meta-rules:** name foundational principles as their own topics; single canonical source (pointer, don't restate — and its design-time cousin: reuse an existing correlation/identity signal before inventing new plumbing); don't defer completable bounded sweeps; graduated verification (confidence, not boolean). See `naming-foundational-principles.md`, `single-canonical-source-discipline.md`, `reuse-existing-correlation-signal.md`, `feedback-don-t-defer-completable-scope.md`, `graduated-verification-confidence.md`.\n- **User-feedback working style:** ranked-shortlist over exhaustive enumeration; confirm before writing durable lore mid-session; in design dialogues, write the draft only when the user triggers it (decisions are safe in conversation — don't repeatedly move to persist); populate dry-run counters with would-be outcomes; \"enforce X\" ≠ add a required schema field; for broad/emotionally-loaded open-ended asks, decompose into hidden axes and sequence a build order by dependency (cheapest/highest-leverage first, flashiest/most-structural last) rather than proposing a menu or jumping to implementation; on a second round of pushback on the same axis (length, tone, scope), act on the next ask instead of re-justifying — a second \"no\" is not a request for more reasoning; a **short measurement question gets a short factual answer**, with the interesting generalisation offered rather than delivered, and **several style skills invoked at once is a stop signal**, not a preference tweak (hold the style for the rest of the session). See `feedback-too-many-words.md`, `feedback-confirm-before-writing-lore.md`, `feedback-draft-only-when-user-triggers.md`, `feedback-schemas-as-enforcement-overreach.md`, `feedback-layered-decomposition-for-open-ended-asks.md`, `feedback-mvp-minimalism.md`, `feedback-comply-promptly-after-repeated-pushback.md`.\n\n## Key Constraints\n\n- `lore-context.md` ≤ 50K tokens; **shape over size** — working-knowledge + summary-topic references, not an index (see `lore-context-shape-discipline.md`).\n- Lore topics: atomic, <5K tokens preferred, plain markdown, no frontmatter.\n- Descriptor frontmatter: `lore-repo.md` = `description` + `version` (+ optional `repos:`); `role.md` = `description` only.\n- Command filenames: lowercase/digits/hyphens, ≤64 chars.\n- Placeholders: `<workspace>`, `<lore-agent-repo>`, `<guest-lore-agent-repo>`, `<agent-name>`, `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`.\n- **CWD safety:** never `cd` when later tools depend on cwd — use `git -C <repo>`. **Portable shell:** assume BSD/macOS, no GNU-only binaries (`timeout`); bound commands via the Bash-tool timeout.\n- See `conventions.md`, `placeholder-vocabulary.md`, `tooling-cwd-safety.md`, `portable-shell-in-framework-docs.md`.\n\n## Onboarding-Doc Authoring\n\nCo-authoring framework onboarding docs for adopting teams is part of the role. Two distinct genres now exist: **`onboarding-doc-narrative-pattern.md`** (long-form prose pitching a human reader) and **`paste-link-installer-doc-genre.md`** (a doc written *to the AI agent* as the literal installer, meant to be pasted as a link — shipped as `QUICKSTART.md` + per-engine `INSTALL-<ENGINE>.md`). Load the identity-layer framings first, then the toolkit: the two genre topics above, `use-cases-via-parallel-consult-pattern.md`, `terminology-domain-collision-trap.md`, `agent-as-universal-working-environment.md`, `in-flight-skill-teaching-pattern.md`. Pre-ship review for either genre uses `parallel-reviewer-fanout-pattern.md`'s multi-lens fan-out; the installer genre additionally needs the **AI-installer (literal executor)** lens (`ai-installer-review-lens.md`) — it catches execution-fidelity bugs (e.g. `skill-doc-filename-divergence-bug-class.md`) the newcomer/editorial lenses miss. Landing-page placement of a self-referential/meta example differs from long-narrative placement — primacy goes to the strongest CTA; see `onboarding-doc-narrative-pattern.md` § placement note. A recurring funnel bug: an author writing from the fresh-start perspective railroads readers into create-your-first-agent and leaves the **team-join path** invisible at every layer (README prose, QUICKSTART, and the INSTALL AI-agent preambles) — check all layers, and keep the fork question verbatim-identical across sites (`onboarding-funnel-team-join-path.md`). Adopter-facing prose carries the product name **\"Lore Agents\"** while the engine keeps `lore-framework`/`lr` (`lore-agents-product-name.md`). First instance: the Activities team's intro doc.\n\n## Active Design Explorations\n\n- **lr-dev / Dark Factory (DF)** — major direction; a `lr` module for SDLC automation toward an autonomous \"dark factory\" SDLC. Per-repo artifacts + narrative context live in a `<repo>-df` backbone (a `repo-lore/<file>/` mirror: `file-lore.md` narrative landing + flat structured aspect subdirs like `ula/`). Skills not agents; persistence external. First aspect: **AIQA/ULA** (`/lr:df-repo-init`, `/lr:df-ula-file`) — unit-level analysis with a bug-verification track, BETA. The DF/ULA design thread is closed and the module ships as BETA. Anchor: `lr-dev-direction.md`; see `df-per-repo-backbone.md`, `aiqa-ula-feature.md`, `df-module-conventions.md`, `workflow-primitive-operational-notes.md`.\n- **Autonomous agents / Lore Beings** — agents as always-on background collaborators with persistent task state, raising for input only when needed. Concrete steps taken: `/lr:spawn-teammate` (multi-agent substrate, v10) and the v18 **`lr-wait`** primitive — the first *inbound-signal* step: an agent blocks on an event and an external actor (cron/CI/webhook/human, via `lr-emit`) wakes it with text. **The beings design is settled (2026-07-19): the module is _Lore Beings_.** A being is an ordinary lore agent plus a `being.md` descriptor; the **Being Keeper** (`lrb`) is deterministic substrate (never an LLM). **MVP is CLI-only**; engines are explicit user config. Budget = daily-USD spawn gate + per-task wall-clock kill. **Engine kinds: `claude`, `codex`, and `cursor`** — cursor landed in framework v28: requires `--plugin-dir` at `engines add`, claude-shaped JSON result + flat-cost fallback. **Keeper-specific real-engine lifecycle coverage now exists** (`tests/lifecycle/keeper_harness.py` + `test_lrb_lifecycle.py`, 10 scenarios after the 2026-07-20 fifth pass added B2/B3, separate higher-blast-radius gate `LR_LIFECYCLE_KEEPER=1`, verified claude 6/6 + codex 1/1 + cursor 1/1 at the recommended-minimum tier). That fifth pass found and fixed a real production bug, not just a coverage gap: `cursor-agent`'s sandboxed shell tool escapes `_kill`'s `killpg` by running spawned commands in a freshly `setsid`'d session, which left a real orphaned process on the test machine before the fix — `_kill` now also walks the full ppid-descendant tree and signals every descendant directly, enumerated *before* any ancestor is signaled (killing the ancestor first risks the OS reparenting a survivor to PID 1 and erasing the ppid link). **The Keeper substrate shipped BETA in v28, and v29 added the in-engine command surface `/lr:being`** — one skill with subcommands (status/init/create/validate/logs/keeper/engine/workspace/pause/resume) over the same deterministic `lrb` CLI, rather than a `lrb-*` skill per operation. Ship record: v28 at framework commit `5e00209`, tag `lr--v1.28.0`; the standard lifecycle gate passed by persisted broad matrix plus targeted reruns. Real-engine verification sharpened two per-kind contract gaps (both backlog schema decisions, not silently patched): the `cursor` kind is empirically cost-blind (no `total_cost_usd`), so its flat `--session-cost-usd` fallback is load-bearing not optional; the `claude` kind has no `--plugin-dir` field, so a claude-kind being needs a wrapper-script `command` to load `lr:` skills at all. Two further findings from that same review pass — budget-enforcement edge cases and an unattended-full-permission trust gap — were deliberately deferred to the backlog rather than fixed. Chronicler week-long soak and persistent `--launchd` install still user-triggered. Open gap: headless permissions (`permission_mode: full` vs future scoped-tools), and self-scheduling under the safe default. Anchor: `lore-beings-design.md`; see `cursor-agent-real-invocation-contract.md`, `engine-kinds-design-decision.md`, `lifecycle-testing-harness.md` § Keeper coverage, `keeper-spawn-prompt-boilerplate-distraction.md`, `lore-beings-mvp-takeover-review.md`, `kill-tree-enumerate-before-signal-ordering.md`, `codex-exec-real-invocation-contract.md`, `macos-ps-o-multi-field-single-line.md`, `agent-being-consciousness-substrate-split.md`, `unenforceable-caps-are-prompt-theater.md`, `feedback-mvp-minimalism.md`, `autonomous-agents-vision.md`, `wait-primitive-feature.md`, `framework-improvements-backlog.md` § Major Directions § Autonomous Agents / Lore Beings.\n- **Multi-engine portability (Codex, Cursor)** — **the ports are shipped, not in flight.** Claude\n  Code, Codex, and Cursor are all Tier-1 supported, so a mixed-engine team shares one agent repo.\n  Claude Code remains the reference path: shared procedure docs are written in Claude terms, and other\n  engines override only at binding points. What made this tractable: the knowledge substrate (agent\n  repos, `lore-repo.md`, `role.md`, `lore/`, `lore-context.md`, git) was already engine-agnostic, so\n  the port was **packaging, not redesign** — the whole surface is **5 adapter bindings** via the\n  `docs/engines/` convention (framework-root, invocation-syntax, subagent-spawn, memory-file,\n  runtime-bounding) plus Boot Step-0 engine selection. Both engines have native in-session subagents\n  (Codex `spawn_agent`/`wait_agent`; Cursor `Task`), so the feared Tier-B nucleus is proven, and one\n  repo carries both skill namespaces (canonical `skills/<skill>/` for Claude, `.cursor-skills/lr-<skill>/`\n  wrappers for Cursor, kept in sync by `scripts/sync-cursor-skills` and `/lr:check` #21).\n\n  Standing operational facts worth carrying: **trust rollout/tool-call logs, not model self-report**,\n  when validating an engine path; Codex's default sandbox blocks `.git` writes and network, so the\n  supported finalization path needs `.git` writable through launch/config (a commit-blocked run is\n  degraded fallback, not a merge failure); Codex per-agent shortcut register/unregister/list remains an\n  unvalidated implementation gap. **Separate sessions on separate engines coordinating on a real task**\n  (not `/lr:attach`, which is single-executor multi-agent) now has a validated substrate: a shared\n  append-only folder (worked example: `workdir/v31-feedback/`), reusable across message-vocabulary\n  changes — see `cross-engine-team-substrate-validated.md`. Two rules fell out of running it for real:\n  **check for same agent identity before any session writes** (two engines can be the same booted\n  agent against the same repo — designate one write-owner, `same-agent-multiple-engines-single-writer.md`)\n  and **don't relay a user decision from one session into the shared channel as settled authority for\n  another session** (`cross-engine-relay-not-attributable-authority.md`). A genuinely different engine\n  also catches design flaws same-engine self-review (even multi-lens) misses — a distinct axis from the\n  sandboxed-review/self-report family, worth the coordination cost on high-stakes design decisions,\n  not routine changes (`independent-engine-review-catches-structural-blind-spots.md`). The **\"framework\n  is prose executed by the model\"** risk is\n  empirically retired for the exercised paths, and the substrate half has quantitative backing — the\n  quality benchmark showed positive lore-utilization uplift on every engine+model config, with the\n  nuance that **model–engine fit beats model tier**. Cross-engine support is a *supporting* fact in\n  positioning, not the headline (the 2026-07-20 landscape re-survey found competitors claiming it too);\n  lead with the triad. Anchor: `multi-engine-portability-direction.md`; per-engine entry points\n  `claude-engine-capabilities.md`, `codex-engine-capabilities.md`, `cursor-engine-capabilities.md`;\n  see also `docs-engines-convention.md`, `subagent-as-optimization-vs-subagent-as-semantics.md`,\n  `claude-coupling-inventory-and-port-tiers.md`, `cursor-dual-skill-tree-one-repo.md`,\n  `quality-benchmark-feature.md`, `positioning-triad-differentiation.md`, `port-landing-next-steps.md`.\n- **Lore housekeeping / consolidation \"sleep\" pass** and the **simplification/subtraction** review item — active follow-ups from the 2026-06-13 architecture review; see `framework-improvements-backlog.md`. That review's settled dispositions (incl. DF-inside-`lr` and team-shared/multi-author as deliberate, not defects — don't re-raise) live in `architecture-review-dispositions.md`. A newer 2026-07-02 review added two further backlog items (post-merge diff verification, recall-time staleness surfacing) — see `framework-improvements-backlog.md` § Merge Quality, § Search / Scaling.\n- Parked: workdir-as-reference-library; vector-DB search (until >100 topics/agent); the session-as-durable-artifact cluster (boot auto-push, boot-context cache, suspend/resume, JSONL archive). All in `framework-improvements-backlog.md`.\n- **v25 workspace layer (pull + init)** — implemented locally in `lore-framework` commit `0311ab6`.\n  Hard renames: workspace-sync→workspace-pull, init→workspace-init. Two-level repo declarations\n  (`lore-workspace.md` + domain `repos:`), optional workspace-as-git-repo envelope, cursor wrapper\n  regeneration, checks #22–23. Full lifecycle remains the pre-push gate. See\n  `v25-workspace-pull-init-design.md`, `workspace-meta-repo-pattern.md`.\n\n## Current State\n\nWorkspace holds three canonical repos: **`lore-framework/`** (plugin), **`lore-framework-dev/`**\n(this repo — lore-architect lore, tests, drafts), and **`lore-agents/`** (personal agents).\n\n`lore-framework/` is **clean and pushed at v31** on `main`. The newest shipped capability surface is\nthe deterministic `lr-core` substrate and Script Fallback Contract; the release also includes the\nreworked `/lr:trilens-loop`, Lore Beings, Markdown session archives, and multi-engine lifecycle\nidentity protections. Three engines are Tier-1 supported — Claude Code, Codex, Cursor.\n\n**v31 was shipped on explicit user direction with a recorded gate waiver, not as release-green.**\nThe remaining Codex `test_07`/`test_08` reruns, full Cursor lifecycle shard, and final trilens gate\nwere intentionally skipped; prior evidence does not cover them. Preserve unrelated dirty-tree\nchanges during any release work, and record any such waiver with the skipped gates in\n`versioning-release-types.md`. See `v31-lr-core-parked-2026-07-25.md`,\n`lifecycle-testing-harness.md` § Release Gate Evidence States, and\n`lifecycle-harness-plugin-identity-unverified.md`.\n\nBoth pre-ship gates are in working order and both are routinely run: the review loop via\n`/lr:trilens-loop`, and the real-engine lifecycle suite via `tests/lifecycle/` (plus the separate\nhigher-blast-radius `LR_LIFECYCLE_KEEPER=1` Keeper track and the `LR_QUALITY=1` benchmark track).\nSee `trilens-loop-feature.md`, `lifecycle-testing-harness.md`, `lore-beings-design.md`,\n`session-summaries-feature.md`.\n\n**v32 — version-agnostic registered boot shortcuts** — locally committed on branch\n`codex/shortcut-bootstrap` in both repos (`lore-framework` `4f35a0f`, `lore-framework-dev`\n`84395f8`), **not yet merged to `main` or pushed**. Generated per-agent shortcuts no longer bake an\nabsolute, version-pinned path to `agent-boot.md`; they self-locate framework authority off the\nsession's actively installed boot skill instead, fixing a defect where a shortcut pinned to a dead\nplugin-cache path after an upgrade. Designed via the three-engine (Claude/Codex/Cursor) shared-folder\nreview recorded at `agents/lore-architect/workdir/v31-feedback/`. Shipped locally under an explicit\nuser waiver — the real per-engine upgrade-lifecycle regression is filed as a required follow-up, not\nproven. See `versioning-release-types.md`'s v32 entry, `cross-engine-team-substrate-validated.md`,\n`fix-the-pointer-not-the-shipped-migration.md`.\n\n## Running Backlog & Standing Improvement List\n\n`framework-improvements-backlog.md` is the canonical list of deferred items; its § Ship Closures\narchives per-ship gate dispositions. Quality benchmark tier/probe expansion is in the dev repo with\nregular/deep matrix defaults and local override support. ~195 lore topics. The\nbacklog is organized into top-level `##` categories (Major Directions, Session Lifecycle &\nDurability, Knowledge Quality & Curation, Multi-Agent Collaboration, Workspace & Environment,\nFramework Upkeep/Distribution/Docs, Ship Closures archive), each holding `###` topical sections —\nthe fix for a flat list that outgrew ~30 sections.     "text": "# Searching Your Lore\n\nRead this when you need to find or recall topics from your lore. Covers both the agent-initiated case (you decide you need lore) and the user-triggered case (`/lr:recall`). Also covers the multi-agent case when guests are attached to the host session.\n\nDefault scope is `agents/<agent>/lore/` only. Do not include `sessions/`, `archive/`, or other agent subdirectories in ordinary lore recall. Search/read `archive/` only when the user explicitly asks for full session logs or a particular session, or when the task specifically requires session-log evidence.\n\n## Preferred Mechanism: Subagent Scan\n\nFor any non-trivial search, **dispatch an `Explore` subagent** with a structured search brief. The subagent reads relevant lore topics directly, synthesizes findings, and returns a compact result. This keeps your main context clean and uses the LLM's own semantic understanding — stronger than keyword matching for finding topics by *meaning* rather than by *exact term*.\n\n> **Engine note.** \"`Explore` subagent\" and \"single-message parallel dispatch\" describe Claude Code. If your engine profile (`<framework-root>/docs/engines/<engine>.md`) defines a subagent-spawn override, use its read-only spawn instead — e.g. on Codex, `spawn_agent` with role `explorer`, gathered via `wait_agent`.\n\nWhy a subagent:\n- Reading 10–100 topic files in your main context wastes tokens you need for the user's task\n- The synthesis can be inlined cheaply (a few hundred words)\n- `Explore` is fast and tool-restricted (Read/Grep/Glob, no edit/agent tools) — exactly what's needed for a search\n\n### Give the subagent a topic manifest\n\nBefore dispatching, generate the reading map:\n\n```\npython3 \"<framework-root>/scripts/lr-core\" scan --agent-dir \"<agent-dir>\"\n```\n\nIt returns every topic with its title line, last-commit date, age in days, and a staleness flag — one git call for the whole directory, rather than the subagent globbing and sampling files to work out what exists. Pass the output to the subagent as part of its brief (write it to a temp file and give the path if it is large; a 150-topic manifest is sizeable, and it belongs in the *subagent's* context, not yours).\n\nTwo things this buys beyond speed:\n\n- **Search by title, not just by filename.** The manifest exposes what each topic actually claims, so the subagent picks what to read on meaning rather than guessing from the name.\n- **Age-awareness.** Lore accumulates for years; a topic last touched 14 months ago may describe a superseded design. Tell the subagent to **note the age of anything it surfaces, and flag findings from `stale: true` topics as needing verification** before they are acted on. Age is a caution flag, not a disqualifier — plenty of old topics are still exactly right.\n\nIf the script fails to complete, apply the **Script Fallback Contract** (`<framework-root>/docs/conventions.md`). Unlike the other call sites, there's no hand-executed equivalent worth running here (read `cmd_scan`'s docstring (the numbered steps; `scan_lore` below it is the implementation) in `<framework-root>/scripts/lr-core` if you want the exact manifest logic) — simply dispatch the subagent without a manifest; it will list and read the directory itself, which is the historical behavior and still works.\n\n## Active Agents: Fan Out When Guests Are Attached\n\nIf guests are attached to the host session (via `/lr:attach`), the search runs across **all active agents' lore directories** — host + every attached guest. Execute this by dispatching **one `Explore` subagent per active agent in parallel** (all subagent calls in a single message), each scoped to one agent's `lore/` directory.\n\nEach subagent receives the same brief but is told which agent's lore to scan. Each returns its own compact synthesis. The host merges the results for the user, grouped by agent so it's clear whose lore each finding came from.\n\nWhen there are no guests, the fan-out reduces to a single subagent — behavior is identical to the single-agent case. You don't need to branch your procedure; just enumerate the active agents (host plus any attached guests) and dispatch N subagents.\n\n**Why parallel, not serial:** each subagent runs independently and there are no dependencies between them. Single-message parallel dispatch is both faster and the standard pattern for independent subagent work.\n\n## How to Prepare a Search Brief\n\nThe quality of what comes back depends entirely on the brief. A good brief has four parts:\n\n### 1. The Task — One or Two Sentences\n\nWhat you're actively working on. Be concrete:\n\n- Good: *\"Refactoring the framework update process to support a release-notes track alongside migrations.\"*\n- Bad: *\"Working on framework stuff.\"*\n\n### 2. Session Context That Shapes Relevance\n\nTwo to five bullets covering decisions made, constraints surfaced, paths or filenames being touched, open questions. This is what lets the subagent distinguish *relevant* from *generally related*.\n\n### 3. The Angle — If You Have One\n\nIf the user provided a hint (`/lr:recall anything about versioning`), center the brief on it.\n\nIf not, describe the *kind* of lore that would help — prior decisions, operational lessons, domain facts, known pitfalls, past trade-offs. This anchors the subagent's reading.\n\n### 4. The Output Shape You Want Back\n\nAsk explicitly for:\n- A compact synthesis (under ~400 words) grouped by theme\n- Each finding tagged with its source topic filename\n- Explicit call-outs for topics worth reading in full yourself\n- *\"Nothing relevant found\"* if that's the honest answer — don't invent relevance\n\nKeep the whole brief under ~300 words. The subagent has access to your full `lore/` directory; you don't need to summarize what's *in* lore, only what's in *this session*.\n\n## Worked Example\n\n**Brief sent to the subagent:**\n\n> Search the lore at `agents/lore-architect/lore/` for topics relevant to my current task.\n>\n> **Task:** Designing a `/lr:recall` skill that lets the user explicitly trigger a lore search mid-session. The synthesis is inlined into the main agent's working context.\n>\n> **Session context:**\n> - Decided against introducing a vector DB at current scale — using LLM-driven subagent scan instead\n> - Framework bumping to v3 with new search guidance and a recall skill\n> - Keeping `agent-boot.md` compact — detailed search guidance lives in a separate doc\n> - Following the existing skill/doc separation pattern\n>\n> **Looking for:** prior decisions on user-invocable skills, transparency patterns (showing intermediate results to the user), past discussion of subagent usage in the framework.\n>\n> **Output:** compact synthesis grouped by theme, ≤400 words, with topic filenames. Flag anything I should read in full. Say \"nothing relevant\" if true.\n\n**Expected response shape:**\n\n> **User-invocable skill conventions** (`slash-command-system.md`): all user-invocable skills use the `/lr:` prefix; generated artifacts use `/lr-`. Skill files are thin pointers; logic lives in `docs/`.\n>\n> **Skill/doc separation** (`skill-doc-pattern.md`): every skill is one line pointing at `<framework-root>/docs/<name>.md`. Recall should follow this exactly.\n>\n> **No prior recall pattern** — no existing topic discusses user-triggered context enrichment. New ground.\n>\n> **Read in full:** `slash-command-system.md` for the conventions you'll be following.\n\n## What to Do With the Result\n\n1. **Show the synthesis to the user.** Transparency — they should see what got pulled in and judge whether the recall was useful. If multiple active agents were scanned (fan-out), group results by agent so it's clear whose lore each finding came from.\n2. **Carry it as working context** for the rest of the session.\n3. **Read flagged topics in full yourself** — don't trust synthesis alone for load-bearing details.\n\n## Direct-Tool Fallback\n\nWhen you know exactly what you're looking for or just need a quick check, the standard tools are fine — no subagent needed:\n\n- **Glob / list** — see what topics exist\n- **Grep** — search for a specific keyword across topics\n- **Read** — read a specific topic in full\n- **`git log`** — when a topic was created (`git -C <lore-agent-repo> log --diff-filter=A -- agents/<agent-name>/lore/<topic>.md`), last updated (`git -C <lore-agent-repo> log -1 -- agents/<agent-name>/lore/<topic>.md`), or full history (`git -C <lore-agent-repo> log --follow -- agents/<agent-name>/lore/<topic>.md`). Use `git -C` rather than `cd`ing into the repo — the shell CWD is shared with Glob and Grep.\n- **Deleted topics** remain in git history — searchable if you need past knowledge that was later removed\n\nRule of thumb: use the subagent when you're searching by **meaning**; Never silently invent a boot procedure.\n   103\t\n   104\tOne engine-profile note that lives here rather than in the script, because it's about which\n   105\t*engine* is running, not about `lr-core`'s logic: if the selected engine profile (Step 0) declares\n   106\tteammate detection **unsupported** or **inapplicable**, skip `detect_teammate` entirely and assume\n   107\ta normal host session — same as passing `--no-teammate-check`.\n   108\t\n   109\t## Your Lore\n   110\t\n   111\tYour lore is stored in the `lore/` directory within your agent directory. It is a collection of plain markdown files — each file is a **lore topic**, an atomic piece of knowledge.\n   112\t\n   113\tLore topics contain:\n   114\t- Decisions and their reasoning\n   115\t- Domain knowledge and discoveries\n   116\t- Operational recommendations from experience\n   117\t- Practical context about the systems and environment you work in\n   118\t\n   119\tLore topics reference each other by filename, forming a knowledge graph. Some topics are **summary topics** that provide an overview of an area and link to more specific topics.\n   120\t\n   121\t### Searching Your Lore\n   122\t\n   123\t`lore-context.md` is a compressed index, not the full picture — treat it as a starting point, not the answer. At the start of any non-trivial task, scan your `lore/` directory for related topics before proceeding. Never act on assumptions about things you might have encountered in previous sessions without first confirming in your lore.\n   124\t\n   125\tDefault lore search means `lore/` only. Do **not** search `sessions/` or `archive/` as part of ordinary recall: `sessions/` contains summaries, and `archive/` contains full session logs that can be overwhelmingly large. Search or read `archive/` only when a human explicitly asks about full session logs / a particular session, or when you have a concrete reason that session-log evidence is important for the task.\n   126\t\n   127\tWhen you need to search or recall lore, read `<framework-root>/docs/lore-search.md` and follow the procedure there.\n   128\t\n   129\t## Your Workdir\n   130\t\n   131\tYour agent directory contains a `workdir/` directory. This is your persistent workspace for files, scripts, tools, and any other artifacts you create or need across sessions.\n   132\t\n   133\tYou decide the internal structure of `workdir/` — organize it however makes sense for your work.\n   134\t\n   135\t## Workspace Visibility\n   136\t\n   137\tYou have access to the entire workspace — all sibling repositories, data, and resources. Your lore is specific to you, but your reach is workspace-wide.\n   138\t\n   139\t## Collaborating with Other Agents\n   140\t\n   141\tThe user may invoke any of three cross-agent mechanisms during the session:\n   142\t\n   143\t- **`/lr:recall [hint]`** — search lore across the **currently loaded** agents (you, plus any attached guests). Fans out to one subagent per active agent. See `<framework-root>/docs/recall.md` and `lore-search.md`.\n   144\t- **`/lr:consult <agent> [hint]`** — ask an **unloaded** agent a focused question. A subagent boots the consultant, answers, and exits. You get back a synthesis plus pointers to specific lore topics or workdir tools you can read or use directly. No finalization for the consultant. See `<framework-root>/docs/consult.md`.\n   145\t- **`/lr:attach <agent>`** — load another agent as a **guest** into this session for sustained co-work. You remain the sole executor (host); the guest's role and lore-context join yours. Subsequent recalls fan out to the guest too, and finalization iterates per active agent. See `<framework-root>/docs/attach.md`.\n   146\t\n   147\tRough rule: recall is for lore you already have loaded; consult is a one-shot question with file handover; attach is for sustained multi-turn work spanning multiple agents' knowledge.\n   148\t\n   149\t## Session Finalization\n   150\t\n   151\tAt the end of a session, when the user triggers finalization, you preserve what you learned. This is a two-step process:\n   152\t\n   153\t1. **Reflection** — extract what's worth keeping into reflection topics. Triggered by `/lr:reflect`.\n   154\t2. **Merge** — a separate step integrates reflections into your lore. Triggered by `/lr:merge`.\n   155\t\n   156\tBoth steps together: `/lr:finalize`.\n   157\t\n   158\tIf guests are attached to this session (via `/lr:attach`), both reflection and merge iterate per active agent in host-first order — each agent learns what fits its role. See `<framework-root>/docs/process-reflection.md` and `process-merge.md` for the iteration mechanics.\n   159\t\n   160\tDo not perform finalization unless the user explicitly triggers it.\n   448\t        pass  # A stamp we cannot write only costs us the TTL optimization.\n   449\t\n   450\t\n   451\tdef pull_repo(repo, ttl=DEFAULT_PULL_TTL_SEC, fresh=False, do_pull=True):\n   452\t    \"\"\"Manual fallback: the exact TTL-cached auto-pull procedure for one repo.\n   453\t\n   454\t    Step 1: if `do_pull` is False (--no-pull), do nothing — report \"disabled\".\n--\n   588\t# Version comparison (detection only — migrations stay with the model)\n   589\t# --------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   590\t\n   591\tdef compare_versions(repo_version, fw_version):\n   592\t    \"\"\"Manual fallback: how to read repo_version against fw_version by hand.\n   593\t\n   594\t    Either missing/unreadable -> \"unknown\". Equal as strings -> \"match\". Else\n--\n   642\t    return val if val else None\n   643\t\n   644\t\n   645\tdef detect_teammate():\n   646\t    \"\"\"Manual fallback: walk process ancestry for the Agent-Teams marker.\n   647\t\n   648\t    Step 1: start at `os.getppid()` (this process's immediate parent).\n--\n   914\t    return res\n   915\t\n   916\t\n   917\tdef _resolve_agent(args, res, workspace):\n   918\t    \"\"\"Manual fallback: locate the target agent by hand.\n   919\t\n   920\t    Step 1: if --agent-dir was given, use it directly. Require a role.md\n--\n   970\t    return matches[0]\n   971\t\n   972\t\n   973\tdef cmd_preflight(args, res):\n   974\t    \"\"\"The boot/attach/consult/merge preflight. This function's steps ARE the\n   975\t    normative procedure (docs/conventions.md § Script Fallback Contract) — if\n   976\t    this script cannot run, read the six steps below (and the referenced\n"

This function's steps ARE the\n   975\t    normative procedure (docs/conventions.md § Script Fallback Contract) — if\n   976\t    this script cannot run, read the six steps below (and the referenced\n   977\t    helper functions for their own exact commands) and execute them by hand.\n   978\t\n   979\t    Step 1: resolve <framework-root> (this script's grandparent dir, or\n   980\t    --framework-root) and read its VERSION file. Missing/unreadable VERSION is\n   981\t    a warning, not a failure — the version check below just becomes\n   982\t    inconclusive.\n   983\t\n   984\t    Step 2: resolve the target agent. If --agent-dir was given, use it\n   985\t    directly: require a role.md there, and derive the repo root by walking up\n   986\t    two directories and checking for lore-repo.md (warn, don't fail, if that\n   987\t    check comes up empty — repo-scoped steps 4-5 below are skipped for a\n   988\t    repo-less agent). Otherwise, discover every repo under <workspace> and\n   989\t    match --agent by name; no match is a failure (report available_agents);\n   990\t    more than one match is a warning (use the first, arbitrarily). See\n   991\t    _resolve_agent below for the exact logic.\n   992\t\n   993\t    Step 3: record the agent's role.md and lore-context.md paths as\n   994\t    `read_next`. Reading and interpreting those files is the caller's job —\n   995\t    their content is judgment material, not something this script parses.\n   996\t\n   997\t    Step 4: if the agent sits under a real lore repo, auto-pull it (TTL-cached\n   998\t    — see pull_repo below for the exact `git pull --ff-only` invocation, the\n   999\t    fast-fail env vars, and the `.git/lr-last-pull` TTL stamp file), then\n  1000\t    compare the repo's lore-repo.md version stamp against the framework\n  1001\t    VERSION read in Step 1 (see compare_versions below for the exact match/\n  1002\t    skew rules). A failed pull is a warning (\"continue in degraded mode\"), not\n  1003\t    a stopping condition. Version skew other than an exact match is also a\n  1004\t    warning pointing at docs/version-check.md.\n  1005\t\n  1006\t    Step 5: otherwise (no enclosing repo — the warning from Step 2), skip both\n  1007\t    of those: report pull as \"skipped\" and version as \"unknown\", and run no\n  1008\t    git subprocess at all.\n  1009\t\n  1010\t    Step 6: detect an Agent-Teams teammate spawn (see detect_teammate below for\n  1011\t    the exact `ps -o args= -p <ppid>` walk and the `--agent-id` flag-boundary\n  1012\t    match), unless --no-teammate-check suppressed it.\n  1013\t    \"\"\"\n  1014\t    workspace = os.path.abspath(args.workspace)\n  1015\t    root = resolve_framework_root(args.framework_root)\n  1016\t    fw_version = framework_version(root)\n  1017\t    if fw_version is None:\n  1018\t        res.warn(\"no readable VERSION at %s — version check will be inconclusive\" % root)\n  1019\t\n  1020\t    res.data[\"framework_root\"] = root\n  1021\t    res.data[\"workspace\"] = workspace\n  1022\t\n  1023\t    agent = _resolve_agent(args, res, workspace)\n  1024\t    if agent is None:\n  1025\t        return res\n  1026\t\n  1027\t    role_file = os.path.join(agent[\"dir\"], \"role.md\")\n  1028\t    lore_context_file = os.path.join(agent[\"dir\"], \"lore-context.md\")\n  1029\t    if not agent[\"has_lore_context\"]:\n  1030\t        res.warn(\"no lore-context.md at %s — agent has no compacted knowledge yet\"\n  1031\t                 % agent[\"dir\"])\n  1032\t\n  1033\t    res.data[\"agent\"] = {\n  1034\t        \"name\": agent[\"name\"],\n  1035\t        \"dir\": agent[\"dir\"],\n  1036\t        \"repo\": agent[\"repo\"],\n  1037\t        \"description\": agent[\"description\"],\n  1038\t        \"role_file\": role_file,\n  1039\t        \"lore_context_file\": lore_context_file if agent[\"has_lore_context\"] else None,\n  1040\t    }\n  1041\t    # The files the caller must read into context next. Reading them is the\n  1042\t    # caller's job: their content is judgment material, not script output.\n  1043\t    res.data[\"read_next\"] = [p for p in (role_file, lore_context_file if\n  1044\t                                         agent[\"has_lore_context\"] else None) if p]\n  1045\t\n  1046\t    if agent[\"repo\"]:\n  1047\t        # Step 4 (repo branch): TTL-cached pull, then version compare.\n  1048\t        res.data[\"pull\"] = pull_repo(\n  1049\t            agent[\"repo\"], ttl=args.ttl, fresh=args.fresh, do_pull=not args.no_pull)\n  1050\t        if res.data[\"pull\"][\"status\"] == \"failed\":\n  1051\t            res.warn(\"auto-pull failed for %s: %s — continue in degraded mode\"\n  1052\t                     % (agent[\"repo\"], res.data[\"pull\"][\"detail\"]))\n  1053\t        repo_version = parse_frontmatter(\n  1054\t            read_text(os.path.join(agent[\"repo\"], \"lore-repo.md\"))).get(\"version\")\n  1055\t        res.data[\"version\"] = compare_versions(repo_version, fw_version)\n  1056\t        verdict = res.data[\"version\"][\"verdict\"]\n  1057\t        # A skew is reported through data.version ONLY — deliberately never as a\n  1058\t        # warning. version-check.md owns the user-facing message for every skew\n  1059\t        # verdict, and on repo-ahead that message is engine-specific: it names the\n  1060\t        # caller's own plugin-refresh commands. A warning here would be a second,\n  1061\t        # blander rendering of the same fact, and a caller whose doc says \"surface\n  1062\t        # warnings in one line each\" will print it and consider the skew handled.\n  1063\t        # That is exactly how the Codex repo-ahead scenario came to print\n  1064\t        # \"version skew: repo=32 framework=31 (repo-ahead)\" and stop, instead of\n  1065\t        # the plugin-refresh remedy. Emit the data; let the doc own the words.\n  1066\t        if verdict == \"unknown\":\n  1067\t            # Not a skew, but not a clean match either: we could not read a\n  1068\t            # stamp. Silence here reads as \"versions agree\", which is a\n  1069\t            # different and unearned claim.\n  1070\t            #\n  1071\t            # Deliberately does NOT point at version-check.md: that procedure\n  1072\t            # reconciles two comparable versions, and here at least one is\n  1073\t            # absent. Callers (agent-boot.md, attach.md) say to report and\n  1074\t            # continue instead, so this message must not send them elsewhere.\n  1075\t            res.warn(\"could not read a version stamp (repo=%s framework=%s) — \"\n  1076\t                     \"version state unverified; continuing without a version check\"\n  1077\t                     % (repo_version, fw_version))\n  1078\t    else:\n  1079\t        # Step 5 (no-repo branch): hardcode skipped/unknown, no subprocess runs.\n  1080\t        res.data[\"pull\"] = {\"status\": \"skipped\", \"detail\": \"no enclosing lore repo\"}\n  1081\t        res.data[\"version\"] = {\"verdict\": \"unknown\", \"repo\": None,\n  1082\t                               \"framework\": fw_version,\n  1083\t                               \"detail\": \"no enclosing lore repo\"}\n  1084\t\n  1085\t    # Step 6: teammate detection, unless suppressed.\n     1\t# Engine Profile — Codex CLI\n     2\t\n     3\tSelected by boot (`agent-boot.md` Step 0) when `<framework-root>` lives under `~/.codex/…`\n     4\t(e.g. `~/.codex/plugins/cache/<marketplace>/lr/<version>/`). Fills the same five bindings as\n     5\t`claude.md` (the reference profile). **Where a value here conflicts with a shared procedure doc,\n     6\tthis profile wins for that step.**\n     7\t\n     8\tEmpirical basis: Codex trials on `codex-cli 0.142.5`, 2026-07-05 — boot, recall, and full\n     9\tfinalize, plus binary ground-truth of the multi-agent subsystem. See lore\n    10\t`codex-cli-plugin-loading-findings.md` and workdir `codex-multiagent-live-capture.md`.\n    11\t\n    12\t## Binding values\n    13\t\n    14\t| Binding | Value on Codex |\n    15\t|---|---|\n    16\t| **framework-root** | `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` is **empty** here — never rely on it. Use the absolute path self-located in Step 0. When you spawn a subagent, **inline that absolute path** into its brief (the subagent cannot self-locate from your context). |\n    17\t| **invocation-syntax** | Codex installs the `SKILL.md` skills natively; a **user** invokes them through Codex's own skill mechanism. Registered per-agent shortcuts are best exposed as **personal skills** in `~/.codex/skills/`, referenced like `$lr-<agent>-agent`, and generated by `/lr:register-agent` or `/lr:register-repo`. **Do not type `/lr:<skill>` as an agent** — in `codex exec` it falls through to the shell and fails. When you need a skill's behavior mid-task, **read `<framework-root>/skills/<skill>/SKILL.md` — it names the correct `docs/*.md` procedure to follow; don't guess the doc filename from the skill name.** |\n    18\t| **subagent-spawn** | Use Codex's native **`spawn_agent`** tool (feature `multi_agent`, on by default). Roles: `worker` (write), `explorer` (read-only). Collect results with `wait_agent` (call it sparingly — only when blocked on a result). **These tools are in-session model actions — they CANNOT be called from a shell command.** Caps: `agents.max_threads` ≈ 6, `agents.max_depth` = 1 (you may spawn; your subagents may not spawn again). See the fan-out override below. |\n    19\t| **memory-file** | `AGENTS.md` (not `CLAUDE.md`). |\n    20\t| **runtime-bounding** | No Bash-tool timeout flag; a long command is bounded by the Codex sandbox / `agents.job_max_runtime_seconds`. Ignore \"set the Bash-tool timeout\" prose. |\n    21\t\n    22\t## Capability gates\n    23\t\n    24\t- **teammate-detection** — `ps -o args= -p $PPID` is **blocked** in Codex's sandbox\n    25\t  (`operation not permitted`). Treat any error as \"not a teammate\" and continue as a normal host\n    26\t  session. Claude Agent-Teams / `spawn-teammate` features are gated off here. (Codex *does* have a\n    27\t  native multi-agent subsystem — see workdir `codex-multiagent-live-capture.md` — but porting\n    28\t  `spawn-teammate` onto it is a separate future task, not part of this profile.)\n    29\t\n    30\t## Registered shortcut bootstrap\n    31\t\n    32\tWhen `/lr:register-agent` or `/lr:register-repo` emits a Codex personal per-agent skill, use this\n    33\texact body after substituting the agent values:\n    34\t\n    35\t```markdown\n    36\tRead the `SKILL.md` for the installed `lr:boot` skill available in this session. Follow its\n    37\tself-location instruction to resolve `<framework-root>`, then read its `docs/agent-boot.md` and\n    38\tboot as agent `<agent-name>` from `<agent-dir>`.\n    39\t```\n    40\t\n    41\tDo not substitute a `~/.codex/plugins/cache/...` path, scan cache directories, or choose the\n    42\tnewest installed version. If the active boot skill is unavailable, report that the Lore plugin is\n    43\tnot loaded; emitted personal skills do not implement a fallback resolver.\n    44\t\n    45\t## Fan-out override (merge / recall / consult)\n    46\t\n    47\tThe shared procedures (`process-merge.md`, `recall.md`, `lore-search.md`, `consult.md`) describe\n    48\tthe Claude fan-out: N parallel `Agent` calls, and each subagent reads the procedure doc itself.\n    49\tOn Codex, run the **same procedure** with these substitutions:\n    50\t\n    51\t1. **Mechanism.** Express fan-out as **`spawn_agent`** calls (one per active agent), not `Agent`\n    52\t   tool calls, and not shell calls. Spawn them to run concurrently; gather with `wait_agent`.\n    53\t   Use role `worker` for merge (needs write), `explorer` for recall (read-only).\n    54\t2. **Host reads the procedure; subagent gets the steps inline.** Codex convention: *the main\n    55\t   agent reads instruction/reference files itself and does not delegate reading skill\n    56\t   instructions to a subagent.* So **you (the host) read the procedure doc** (`process-merge.md`\n    57\t   etc.) and put the concrete steps into each subagent's brief — instead of telling the subagent\n    58\t   to open the doc. The subagent still **boots as its target agent** (loading that agent's\n    59\t   `role.md` + `lore-context.md` is identity loading, not skill-reading, and stays).\n    60\t3. **Inline the resolved `<framework-root>`** absolute path into every brief (see framework-root\n    61\t   binding).\n    62\t4. **Respect the caps.** Depth-1 fan-out is fine (host → subagents, no re-spawn). If a session\n    63\t   ever has more active agents than `max_threads` (≈6), spawn in chunks and note the chunking —\n    64\t   do not silently cap coverage.\n    65\t\n    66\t## Git & the sandbox (operational)\n    67\t\n    68\tCodex's `workspace-write` sandbox **blocks writes to `.git/`** even inside the working directory\n    69\t(`Operation not permitted` on `.git/index.lock`, `.git/FETCH_HEAD`, …). Consequences:\n    70\t\n    71\t- **auto-pull** (boot) will fail its `git pull` — that is fine, boot already degrades on pull\n    72\t  failure; nothing to fix.\n    73\t- **Lore's supported finalize path on Codex is: run with `.git` writable.** The plugin cannot\n    74\t  widen Codex's sandbox by itself, so this must come from how Codex is launched or configured.\n    75\t  For trusted local use, either run with `--sandbox danger-full-access` or add the repo's `.git`\n    76\t  to the writable roots (`-c sandbox_workspace_write.writable_roots=[…]`). Under the default\n    77\t  sandbox, finalize may still merge and write lore files, but the `git commit` step will be\n    78\t  blocked; treat that as a degraded fallback, not the intended product path.\n    79\t\n    80\t## Plugin refresh (operational)\n    81\t\n    82\tCodex installs `lr` persistently under `~/.codex/plugins/cache/...`. Updating the framework source\n    83\tor marketplace does **not** by itself move the installed plugin version already recorded in Codex.\n    84\t\n    85\t- **Git marketplace:** run `codex plugin marketplace upgrade lore-framework`, then\n    86\t  `codex plugin add lr@lore-framework`.\n    87\t- **Local-source marketplace:** run `codex plugin add lr@lore-framework`.\n    88\t- **Fallback reinstall:** if the installed version still does not move forward, run\n    89\t  `codex plugin remove lr@lore-framework` and then `codex plugin add lr@lore-framework`.\n    90\t\n    91\tA refresh done mid-session affects future sessions, not the one already running. Restart Codex\n    92\tafter the refresh. See `<framework-root>/INSTALL-CODEX.md` for the user-facing install guide and\n    93\t`<framework-root>/scripts/codex-refresh-plugin` for a helper script.\n    94\t\n    95\t## Notes\n    96\t\n    97\t- All values above are **facts to follow**, so you do not rediscover them at runtime.\n    98\t- This profile targets the **stable** `multi_agent` feature only. `multi_agent_v2` /\n    99\t  `enable_fanout` are under development and off by default — do not depend on them.\n"

Print DONE only \"\n    \"after those paths exist.\"\n)\n\nINIT_PROMPT = (\n    \"Invoke the lr:workspace-init skill in this directory. Print DONE when complete.\"\n)\n\nWORKSPACE_PULL_PROMPT = (\n    \"Invoke the lr:workspace-pull skill in this workspace. In your report, explicitly list any broken lore topic or \"\n    \"lore-context.md cross-references you find, by filename. Focus on the workspace's \"\n    \"own agent repos — you do not need to reproduce every check section verbatim.\"\n)\n\nUPDATE_DRYRUN_PROMPT = (\n    \"Invoke the lr:update skill with --dry-run in this workspace. \"\n    \"Print the full report verbatim.\"\n)\n\nREGISTER_AGENT_PROMPT = (\n    f\"Invoke the lr:register-agent skill to create a direct boot shortcut for agent \"\n    f\"'{AGENT_NAME}' in repo '{REPO_NAME}'. Print DONE when complete.\"\n)\n\nREGISTER_REPO_PROMPT = (\n    f\"Invoke the lr:register-repo skill to create direct boot shortcuts for every agent in \"\n    f\"repo '{REPO_NAME}'.
