Print DONE when complete.\"\n)\n\n# trilens-loop: both scenarios bound the loop to a single round so the run stays cheap.\n# The reviewable change is planted by plant_reviewable_change() and left uncommitted.\n_TRILENS_TAIL = (\n    \"When the skill finishes, print exactly these lines:\\n\"\n    \"LENSES: <lens 1> | <lens 2> | <lens 3>\\n\"\n    \"FINDINGS: <one line per finding, naming the file or topic filename it concerns>\\n\"\n    \"TRILENS-DONE\\n\"\n    \"Do not reflect, merge, finalize, commit, or push.\"\n)\n\nTRILENS_PROMPT = (\n    f\"Invoke the lr:boot skill to boot as lore agent '{AGENT_NAME}'. \"\n    \"This workspace has uncommitted changes that need reviewing. Invoke the \"\n    \"lr:trilens-loop skill with this free-text amendment: \\\"one round only\\\". \"\n    \"Follow the procedure exactly, including applying the fixes you accept. \"\n    + _TRILENS_TAIL\n)\n\nTRILENS_REPORT_ONLY_PROMPT = (\n    f\"Invoke the lr:boot skill to boot as lore agent '{AGENT_NAME}'. \"\n    \"This workspace has uncommitted changes that need reviewing. Invoke the \"\n    \"lr:trilens-loop skill with this free-text amendment: \\\"one round only, no fixes \"\n    \"— report the findings but do not edit any file\\\". Follow the procedure exactly. \"\n    + _TRILENS_TAIL\n)\n\nTAKEOVER_CURSOR_FACT = BUILD_TOOL_FACT\n\n\ndef takeover_cursor_prompt(jsonl_path, digest_path):\n    return (\n        f\"Read '{FRAMEWORK_DIR}/docs/takeover.md' and follow the **direct takeover** \"\n        \"procedure (the section for a session id or path). Use this absolute JSONL path \"\n        f\"as the session token:\\n  {jsonl_path}\\n\"\n        \"Run scripts/session-takeover with that path and -o \"\n        f\"'{digest_path}'. Read the digest file fully into context. Then print:\\n\"\n        \"TAKEOVER-OK\\n\"\n        f\"RECALL-FACT: {TAKEOVER_CURSOR_FACT}\\n\"\n        \"TOOL-PAIRING: <tool_results_paired>/<tool_calls_total> from digest metadata\\n\"\n        \"Do not boot, reflect, merge, finalize, commit, or push.\"\n    )\n\n\ndef _codex_boot_prompt(agent_name, extra):\n    \"\"\"Codex's reliable path is doc-driven rather than slash-skill dispatch.\"\"\"\n    return (\n        f\"Read the file at '{FRAMEWORK_DIR}/docs/agent-boot.md' and boot as lore agent \"\n        f\"'{agent_name}'. Follow the boot procedure exactly. {extra}\"\n    )\n\n\ndef codex_prompt(prompt):\n    \"\"\"Translate engine-neutral harness prompts into the Codex doc-driven path.\"\"\"\n    if isinstance(prompt, str) and prompt.startswith(\"PLUGIN-IDENTITY-PROBE\"):\n        # Must not inject FRAMEWORK_DIR — the probe is what detects a wrong install.\n        return (\n            \"Identify which lore-framework / lr plugin Codex has installed for this \"\n            \"machine (the enabled lr@lore-framework plugin / its marketplace source), \"\n            \"not a path from this prompt. Read that plugin's VERSION file. \"\n            \"Print exactly two lines and stop:\\n\"\n            \"FRAMEWORK-ROOT: <absolute path of that plugin root>\\n\"\n            \"PLUGIN-VERSION: <version string>\\n\"\n            \"If no lr plugin is installed, print PLUGIN-IDENTITY-FAILED: no lr plugin installed.\"\n        )\n    if isinstance(prompt, str) and \"docs/takeover.md\" in prompt:\n        return prompt\n    if isinstance(prompt, str) and \"docs/agent-boot.md\" in prompt:\n        return prompt  # already doc-driven (e.g. boot_prompt_for_framework)\n    if prompt == BOOT_PROMPT:\n        return _codex_boot_prompt(\n            AGENT_NAME,\n            \"After boot completes, print two lines:\\n\"\n            \"BOOT-CODEWORD: <the boot codeword stated in your role>\\n\"\n            \"CONTEXT-CODEWORD: <the context codeword stated in your lore-context>\\n\"\n            \"If boot fails, print BOOT-FAILED followed by the reason. \"\n            \"Do not reflect, merge, finalize, commit, or push.\",\n        )\n    if prompt == RECALL_PROMPT:\n        return _codex_boot_prompt(\n            AGENT_NAME,\n            f\"Once booted, read '{FRAMEWORK_DIR}/docs/recall.md' and follow it with hint \"\n            \"\\\"build tool\\\" to search your lore. Print the synthesis verbatim. \"\n            \"Do not reflect, merge, finalize, commit, or push.\",\n        )\n    if prompt == REFLECT_PROMPT:\n        return _codex_boot_prompt(\n            AGENT_NAME,\n            \"During this session you learned a new fact: the fixture project's deployment \"\n            f\"tool is called '{REFLECT_TOOL_CANARY}', replacing the previous tool \"\n            \"'ristretto-ci' because of faster rollback times. Then read \"\n            f\"'{FRAMEWORK_DIR}/docs/process-reflection.md' and follow it to reflect on this \"\n            \"session and capture that fact as a reflection topic. After reflection completes, \"\n            \"print DONE. Do not merge, finalize, commit, or push.\",\n        )\n    if prompt == MERGE_PROMPT:\n# Register / Unregister Agent Shortcuts\n\nManage direct per-agent boot shortcuts for the current engine.\n\nThese shortcuts are **optional** — agents can always be loaded via `/lr:boot <agent-name>` (or\nthe engine-native equivalent). Registration adds a faster direct entry point with an absolute agent\npath and, on skill-based engines, richer routing metadata.\n\nThe four user-facing operations share one procedure doc:\n\n- **Register Agent** — create or refresh a shortcut for one specific agent\n- **Register Repo** — create or refresh shortcuts for every agent in a repo\n- **Unregister Agent** — remove one agent's shortcut\n- **Unregister Repo** — remove every shortcut associated with a repo\n\nUse the current engine profile (`<framework-root>/docs/engines/<engine>.md`, selected at boot) to\ndecide which native artifact to generate.\n\n## Engine-native shortcut locations\n\n- **Claude Code** — workspace-local command file:\n  `.claude/commands/lr-<agent-name>-agent.md`\n- **Cursor** — workspace-local skill:\n  `.cursor/skills/lr-<agent-name>-agent/SKILL.md`\n- **Codex** — personal skill:\n  `~/.codex/skills/lr-<agent-name>-agent/SKILL.md`\n\nAll generated shortcuts must remain thin delegations to the active framework boot entry point.\nThey pin only the agent identity and absolute agent directory; they must never bake a plugin-cache\npath, scan for a framework checkout, select an installed version, or inline boot logic.\n\n## Shared helper steps\n\n### Resolve the target repo\n\nWhen an operation targets a repo explicitly:\n\n1. Treat the repo argument as a directory name relative to the current working directory.\n2. Verify `<workspace>/<repo-name>/lore-repo.md` exists.\n3. Call that path `<lore-agent-repo>`.\n\nWhen an operation targets a single agent and no repo argument was provided:\n\n1. Scan all directories in the current working directory for lore agent repos (directories\n   containing `lore-repo.md` at the root).\n2. Look for `agents/<agent-name>/role.md` inside each repo.\n3. If exactly one match exists, use that repo.\n4. If no match exists, report the available agents and stop with an error.\n5. If multiple matches exist, ask the user which repo they want.\n\n### Resolve agent metadata\n\nFor every target agent:\n\n1. Verify `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/<agent-name>/role.md` exists.\n2. Resolve:\n   - **`<agent-dir>`** — absolute path to `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/<agent-name>/`\n   - **`<repo-name>`** — basename of `<lore-agent-repo>`\n3. Read the `description` field from `role.md` YAML frontmatter. Use it as\n   **`<agent-purpose>`**. If missing, fall back to `Lore agent in <repo-name>`.\n\n### Resolve the shortcut bootstrap\n\nRead the current engine profile's **Registered shortcut bootstrap** section. Copy its exact\nengine-specific bootstrap sentence into every generated shortcut, substituting `<agent-name>` and\n`<agent-dir>`. The active boot skill self-locates the framework root, so the emitted shortcut must\nnot contain an `<agent-boot-path>` or any other plugin-install path.\n\n### Current shortcut templates\n\nGenerate exactly these engine-native forms.\n\n#### Claude Code\n\nWrite exactly this single line plus a trailing newline:\n\n```markdown\n<shortcut-bootstrap>\n```\n\n#### Cursor\n\nWrite this `SKILL.md`:\n\n```markdown\n---\nname: lr-<agent-name>-agent\ndescription: \"Boot the <agent-name> lore agent from <repo-name> — <agent-purpose>\"\npaths:\n  - \"<repo-name>/**\"\ndisable-model-invocation: true\n---\n\n<shortcut-bootstrap>\n```\n\nThe `paths:` scoping keeps the shortcut visible only when the matching repo is relevant in the\nworkspace, and `disable-model-invocation: true` keeps it explicit-only.\n\n#### Codex\n\nWrite this `SKILL.md`:\n\n```markdown\n---\nname: lr-<agent-name>-agent\ndescription: \"Boot the <agent-name> lore agent from <repo-name> — <agent-purpose>\"\n---\n\n<shortcut-bootstrap>\n```\n\n## Register Agent\n\n**Inputs:** `[<lore-agent-repo>] <agent-name>`\n\n1. Resolve `<lore-agent-repo>` and the agent metadata using the shared helper steps above.\n2. Compute the engine-native target path for that agent.\n3. If the target artifact already exists:\n   - If it already points at the same `<agent-dir>`, overwrite it with the current template\n     (refresh behavior).\n   - If it points at a different repo/agent path, warn about the collision and stop without\n     overwriting.\n4. Create the parent directory if needed and write the current template.\n5. Report the created shortcut in the engine-native form:\n   - **Claude Code / Cursor:** `/lr-<agent-name>-agent`\n   - **Codex:** `$lr-<agent-name>-agent`\n\n## Register Repo\n\n**Input:** `<lore-agent-repo>`\n\n1. Resolve `<lore-agent-repo>`.\n2. Scan `<lore-agent-repo>/agents/` for directories containing `role.md`.\n3. For each agent found, run the **Register Agent** procedure above.\n4. Report the created or refreshed shortcuts.\n\n## Unregister Agent\n\n**Inputs:** `[<lore-agent-repo>] <agent-name>`\n\n1. Resolve `<lore-agent-repo>` and `<agent-name>`.\n2. Compute the engine-native shortcut path for that agent.\n3. If the artifact does not exist, report `not registered` and stop successfully.\n4. Delete the artifact:\n   - **Claude Code:** delete the `.md` file.\n   - **Cursor / Codex:** delete the `lr-<agent-name>-agent/` directory.\n5. Report the removed shortcut in the engine-native form.\n\n## Unregister Repo\n\n**Input:** `<lore-agent-repo>`\n\n1. Resolve `<lore-agent-repo>`.\n2. Scan the repo's `agents/` directory for valid agents.\n3. For each agent found, run the **Unregister Agent** procedure above.\n4. Report the removed shortcuts.\n\n## Collision rule\n\nShortcuts are keyed by agent name, so collisions are possible if two repos both define\n`agents/researcher/`.\n\n- If a shortcut already exists and points at a different `<agent-dir>`, do **not** overwrite it.\n- Tell the user which existing path owns the shortcut today and which repo they attempted to\n  register.\n- The user resolves the naming collision by renaming an agent or unregistering the old shortcut\n  first.\n"
  }
]

The `/lr:update` process (and boot-time version-check) stamps the repo version only after all migrations for that repo succeed.\n32\n# Update\n\nReconcile user-side state with the currently-installed framework version.\n\n**Scope:** this command does NOT update the plugin itself. Use your engine's own plugin mechanism for that. `/lr:update` only migrates local artifacts — per-agent shortcuts, repo files, and agent files — to match whatever framework version is currently installed.\n\n## Input\n\nOptional argument: `--dry-run` — print what would change without writing any files. No frontmatter updates, no regenerations, no deletions. Use this to preview before applying.\n\n## Core Concept\n\nA single repo-level version identifier is the source of truth. The framework version lives in `<framework-root>/VERSION`. Each lore agent repo stamps this version in its `lore-repo.md` frontmatter. When the framework is ahead of a repo, `/lr:update` walks intermediate versions in sequence — applying migrations from `<framework-root>/migrations/` and displaying release notes from `<framework-root>/release-notes/` — then stamps the new version, but only after all steps succeed.\n\nThere is no per-agent version stamp. Agents within a repo migrate together with the repo.\n\n## Migrations vs Release Notes\n\nA version bump may carry one or both of:\n\n- **Migration** (`migrations/<N>.md`) — physical instructions to modify user-side files (frontmatter changes, file regenerations, directory restructures). Consumed and executed by the update process.\n- **Release notes** (`release-notes/<N>.md`) — informational content describing what's new in version `N`. Displayed to the user; no file modifications.\n\nVersions that need both have both files. Versions that introduce features without requiring user-side file changes have only release notes. Versions that quietly fix things may have only a migration. **At least one must exist for any version bump** — otherwise the update process treats it as a packaging bug.\n\n## Flow\n\n### 1. Read framework version\n\nRead `<framework-root>/VERSION`. Trim whitespace. This is the target version `F`.\n\n### 2. Discover repos\n\nScan all directories in the current working directory for directories containing `lore-repo.md` at the root. Each is a lore agent repo.\n\nIf no repos are found, report \"no lore agent repos in this workspace\" and stop.\n\n### 3. For each repo, determine migration state\n\nRead the `version` field from `lore-repo.md` frontmatter → repo version `R`.\n\n- **If `R == F`**: report `<lore-agent-repo>: already at version F`, skip.\n- **If `R > F`**: warn `<lore-agent-repo>: stamped as version R, but framework is at F — plugin may be out of date`. Do not migrate this repo.\n- **If `R < F`**: this repo needs migration. Continue to step 4.\n\n### 4. Apply migrations and release notes in order\n\nFor `v` in `R+1` through `F` inclusive:\n\n1. **Migration**: if `<framework-root>/migrations/<v>.md` exists, read it and follow its instructions, scoped to the current repo. The migration doc is plain markdown; interpret its steps and execute them.\n\n2. **Release notes**: if `<framework-root>/release-notes/<v>.md` exists, read it and display the contents to the user.\n\n3. **At least one** must exist for version `v`. If neither `migrations/<v>.md` nor `release-notes/<v>.md` is present, this is a framework packaging bug — report it and stop the upgrade for this repo.\n\n4. If a migration step fails, stop the upgrade for this repo. Do **not** apply any further versions to this repo. Do **not** stamp the new version. Report the failure with enough context for the user to investigate.\n\n### 5. Stamp the new version\n\nOnly after all upgrade steps in step 4 succeed:\n\n1. Read `lore-repo.md` frontmatter.\n2. Update the `version` field to `F` (quoted string).\n3. Write the file back, preserving the rest of the frontmatter and the body.\n\nIf any upgrade step failed in step 4, skip this step — leave the repo at its previous version so the next `/lr:update` run can retry.\n\n### 6. Report\n\nFor each repo processed, print one line per outcome:\n- `<lore-agent-repo>: upgraded from R to F` (success)\n- `<lore-agent-repo>: already at version F` (skipped, current)\n- `<lore-agent-repo>: stamped as R, framework is F — plugin may be out of date` (warning)\n- `<lore-agent-repo>: upgrade to v failed: <reason>` (error)\n\nAt the end, print a summary: total repos, upgraded, skipped, warned, failed.\n\n## Dry-Run Mode\n\nIf `--dry-run` is passed:\n\n- Follow the flow above, but do NOT write any files. No frontmatter updates. No regenerations. No deletions.\n- For each step that would modify a file, print what would change:\n  - `would create: <path>`\n  - `would modify: <path>` — include a unified diff preview\n  - `would delete: <path>`\n- For the version stamp step, print `would stamp <lore-agent-repo>: version R → F`.\n- For manual-edit detection, report `manual edits detected in: <path>` and describe what a merge would propose, but do NOT prompt the user.\n- At the end, print the same summary format as normal mode, prefixed with `[DRY RUN]`.\n\n## Handling Manual Edits to Generated Files\n\nMigrations may need to regenerate files the framework owns as templated output — for example,\nper-agent shortcuts (`.claude/commands/lr-*-agent.md` on Claude Code,\n`.cursor/skills/lr-*-agent/SKILL.md` on Cursor, or `~/.codex/skills/lr-*-agent/SKILL.md` on\nCodex). If a user has manually edited such a file, naive regeneration would destroy their edits.\nHandle this case explicitly.\n\n### Divergence detection\n\nFor a file the current migration intends to overwrite:\n\n1. Compute what the **previous-version** template would have generated for this file (the migration doc provides the known previous templates).\n2. If the on-disk content matches any known previous-version template exactly, the file is **clean** — safely overwrite with the new content.\n3. If the on-disk content does not match any known previous template, the file has been **manually edited** — go to merge handling.\n\n### Merge handling\n\nWhen manual edits are detected:\n\n1. Compute three pieces of content:\n   - `old`: what the previous-version template would have generated (choose the closest match, or the latest known template for that version if multiple exist)\n   - `new`: what the current template generates\n   - `current`: the on-disk content (with the user's manual edits)\n\n2. Attempt a three-way merge: apply the diff from `old` → `new` onto `current`. If the merge is clean (no conflicts), produce a merged result.\n\n3. Present the situation to the user:\n   - Show the path of the file\n   - Show a short description of what the user's edits appear to be (compared to `old`)\n   - Show the suggested merged content\n   - Offer three choices:\n     - **Accept** — write the merged content\n     - **Edit** — user provides their own merged content\n     - **Skip** — leave the file as-is and continue with other migration steps (this may leave the repo in a partially migrated state; warn the user)\n\n4. Proceed based on the user's choice. Record which files required manual resolution in the final report.\n\nIn **dry-run mode**, detect divergence and describe the proposed merge, but do not prompt — just report `manual edits detected: <path>`.\n\n## Commit Policy\n\nDo not run git commands that modify state. Leave all changes uncommitted so the user can review with `git diff` and commit themselves. This applies to both the migrated files and the version stamp in `lore-repo.md`.\n\nIt is acceptable to run read-only git commands (`git -C <lore-agent-repo> status`, `git -C <lore-agent-repo> diff`, `git -C <lore-agent-repo> log`) during migration if needed for context, where `<lore-agent-repo>` is the path of the repo currently being processed. Always use `git -C` rather than `cd`ing into each repo — `/lr:update` iterates across repos, and a stray `cd` will silently shift the CWD for subsequent Glob/Grep/git calls on the next repo.\n\n## Ordering Rules\n\n- **Within a repo**, apply upgrades in strict version order (`R+1`, `R+2`, …, `F`). Never skip versions.\n- **Between repos**, process independently. A failure in one repo does not affect others.\n- **Within a migration**, follow the steps in the order given by the migration doc.\n\n## Error Handling\n\nIf the framework version file (`<framework-root>/VERSION`) is missing or unreadable, stop with a clear error — the plugin is broken or not installed.\n\nIf neither a migration doc nor a release-notes doc exists for a version in the range `R+1 ... F`, stop upgrading that repo and report the gap — this is a framework packaging bug.\n\nIf a migration step throws an error (file not found, permission denied, malformed YAML, etc.), stop upgrading that repo and report the specific failure. Other repos continue.\n\n## What `/lr:update` Does NOT Do\n\n- Does not update the plugin itself (that's Claude Code's job).\n- Does not touch lore topics, `lore-context.md`, or `workdir/` contents — those are agent-owned and not templated.\n- Does not commit changes.\n- Does not auto-resolve manual edits without user confirmation.\n- Does not skip versions.\n- Does not diagnose runtime/environmental issues — if a skill expected from a freshly-applied version isn't appearing, an old skill lingers, or behavior reflects the prior version after a successful update, see `/lr:doctor`. Plugin-cache staleness is the most common cause and is a known ailment.\n.worktrees/lore-framework/shortcut-bootstrap/scripts/lr-core:24:migrations, deciding what lore means, composing summaries, resolving\n.worktrees/lore-framework/shortcut-bootstrap/scripts/lr-core:588:# Version comparison (detection only — migrations stay with the model)\n.worktrees/lore-framework/shortcut-bootstrap/scripts/lr-core:603:    Applying migrations is judgment work: docs/version-check.md.\n.worktrees/lore-framework/shortcut-bootstrap/docs/version-check.md:36:that either skips a needed migration or tells the user to reinstall a perfectly current plugin.\n.worktrees/lore-framework/shortcut-bootstrap/docs/version-check.md:94:   - If `<framework-root>/migrations/<v>.md` exists, look for a `## Write Paths` section (see `conventions.md` § Migration Write Paths). A future workspace-aware gate would close the hole; until then, treat workspace-root paths as protected by the in-migration three-way merge alone.\n333-\n334:If a migration ships without a `## Write Paths` section, the gate falls back to the conservative blanket-dirty rule for any version range that includes that migration. This is a real friction cost for users — every dirty file (including unrelated `workdir/*` runtime state) blocks the auto-upgrade. **Treat the section as mandatory** when authoring a new migration. `/lr:check` #20 enforces this automatically; reviewers should treat a check #20 failure as a blocker.\n335-\n336-### Glob token grammar\n337-\n338-Inside the fenced body, lines that aren't blank, comments, or the `(none)` sentinel are **glob tokens**. The canonical grammar (referenced verbatim by `version-check.md` Step 1b's parser and `check.md` #20.3's validator):\n339-\n340-- A glob token is a **single contiguous run of path-segment characters** with no internal whitespace. Accepted characters: `A-Z`, `a-z`, `0-9`, `.`, `_`, `-`, `/`, `*`, `?`, `[`, `]`, `!`, `\\`.\n341-- Use `**` for arbitrary path depth, `*` for a single path segment, `?` for any single character, `[...]` for a character class, `!` to negate a class, `\\` to escape a literal special character.\n342-- Optionally followed by whitespace and a `# <comment>` to end of line. The trailing comment is stripped; the token is what's left.\n343-- A line containing **internal whitespace before any `#`** is prose, not a glob — the parser treats it as malformed (see `version-check.md` Step 1b on the parser's behavior; `check.md` #20.3 flags it as an error).\n344-\n345-Examples:\n346-\n347-```\n348-agents/*/role.md                         # frontmatter rewrite (valid: single token + comment)\n349-agents/**/*.md                           # arbitrary depth (valid)\n350-.claude/commands/lr-[a-z]*-agent.md      # character class (valid)\n351-agents/[!l]*/role.md                     # negated character class (valid)\n352-```\n353-\n354-Non-examples (would be flagged):\n355-\n356-```\n357:This migration writes role.md files     # internal whitespace, no path-token shape — prose\n358-agents/*/role.md and lore-context.md     # two tokens on one line — split into two\n359-```\n360-\n361-### Empty write-sets — sentinel forms\n362-\n363:If a migration genuinely writes nothing on disk (purely informational, all output is stdout), still include the `## Write Paths` section. The body must declare empty explicitly via one of these accepted forms (this is the canonical grammar — `version-check.md` Step 1b's parser and `check.md` #20.3's validator both reference this section):\n364-\n365-- **Bare sentinel:** a line containing only the literal token `(none)`.\n366-- **Sentinel + prose:** a line starting with `(none)` followed by a space and any free-text comment. The separator can be a bare space, an ASCII hyphen `-`, or an em-dash `—` — all three are accepted.\n367-- **Empty fenced block:** the fenced body contains zero lines after stripping blanks and `#`-comments.\n368-\n369-All three are equivalent: parser contributes nothing to the write-set; gate proceeds without deferral.\nFILE: .worktrees/lore…2122 tokens truncated… path — strip the trailing comment).\n     2. **Sentinel detection**: if any remaining line matches the `(none)` sentinel as defined in `conventions.md` § Migration Write Paths § *Empty write-sets — sentinel forms* (the canonical grammar — do not restate the accepted forms here), this is the **explicit empty write-set sentinel**. Contribute nothing for this version. Do **not** treat `(none)` as a glob, and do **not** fall back to blanket-dirty.\n     3. **Glob lines**: every other remaining line is a repo-relative glob — add to the write-set.\n     4. **Zero remaining lines** (after blanks/comments stripped, and no `(none)` sentinel) is also an explicit empty write-set — same treatment as the sentinel.\n   - If a migration exists but the `## Write Paths` **section is absent entirely** (the heading itself is missing), treat its write-set as **unknown** — fall back to the conservative gate: defer on **any** dirty tracked file (the historical behavior, but only for this version range, not as a global default). Note: an empty fenced block is *not* the same as a missing section — the section's presence with empty content is an explicit declaration of \"writes nothing\" and proceeds without deferral.\n   - If only `release-notes/<v>.md` exists for that version (no migration), it contributes nothing to the write-set — release-notes-only versions don't write to user repos.\n\n#### 1c. Intersect dirty tracked files with the write-set\n\nMatch each dirty tracked file's repo-relative path against the write-set globs.\n\n- **Empty intersection** (no dirty file collides) → proceed to Step 2.\n\n- **Exact `lore-repo.md`-only collision on a stamp-only upgrade** → proceed to Step 2. If the\n  colliding set is exactly `lore-repo.md`, and the computed write-set is also exactly\n  `lore-repo.md` (that is: no migration in `R+1 .. F` writes any repo file beyond the final\n  version stamp), do **not** defer. This is the common \"release-notes-only versions plus a dirty\n  stamp file\" case. It is safe to continue because Step 3 reads the current working-tree\n  `lore-repo.md` and updates only its `version` field while preserving the rest of the file.\n  This also covers a previously-deferred local stamp such as committed `17`, working tree `18`,\n  now advancing to `23`: the earlier dirty stamp is not a reason to block the later stamp-only\n  upgrade.\n\n- **Non-empty intersection** → defer. Print a precise message naming the colliding files and the right command. Substitute `<lore-agent-repo>` with the actual repo path and quote it if it contains spaces — emit literal commands, not template placeholders:\n\n  ```\n  <lore-agent-repo>: cannot auto-upgrade from R to F — uncommitted changes in files the upgrade would write:\n    <file-1>\n    <file-2>\n    ...\n  Resolve by either committing the changes:\n    git -C \"<lore-agent-repo>\" commit <files>\n  or reverting them:\n    git -C \"<lore-agent-repo>\" checkout -- <files>\n  Then boot again or run /lr:update.\n  ```\n\n  **This deferral is NOT a boot failure.** Print the message above, then return to `agent-boot.md` and finish loading the agent in degraded mode — do not emit any boot-failed signal or stop the boot.\n\nIn deferred cases: do NOT modify any files; continue boot in degraded mode. **A deferred upgrade is not a boot failure** — return to `agent-boot.md` Step 2 and proceed to Step 3; the agent still loads.\n\n### Step 2: Walk versions from R+1 to F\n\nFor each version `v` in `R+1` through `F` inclusive, in order:\n\n1. **Apply migration** if `<framework-root>/migrations/<v>.md` exists. Read the doc and follow its instructions, scoped to the booting agent's repo. Migrations are idempotent and may modify files.\n\n2. **Display release notes** if `<framework-root>/release-notes/<v>.md` exists. Print the contents to the user so they see what's new in this version.\n\n3. **At least one** must exist for version `v`. If neither `migrations/<v>.md` nor `release-notes/<v>.md` is present, this is a framework packaging bug — print an error, stop the upgrade for this repo, do NOT stamp the new version, continue boot in degraded mode.\n\n4. If a migration step fails, stop the upgrade for this repo. Do NOT apply any further versions. Do NOT stamp the new version. Print the failure with enough context for the user to investigate. Continue boot in degraded mode.\n\n### Step 3: Stamp the new version\n\nOnly after all versions in step 2 succeed:\n\n1. Read `lore-repo.md` frontmatter from the booting agent's repo.\n2. Update the `version` field to `F` (quoted string).\n3. Write the file back, preserving the rest of the frontmatter and the body.\n\n### Step 4: Inform the user\n\nPrint a brief summary:\n- `<lore-agent-repo>: upgraded from R to F`\n- The changes are uncommitted and ready for `git diff` review\n\nThen return to `agent-boot.md` Step 3 and continue with reading `role.md` and `lore-context.md`.\n\n## Invariants\n\n- **Boot never fails on version errors.** Any failure here results in degraded-mode boot with a visible warning. The agent still loads.\n- **No file the upgrade would write is overwritten while dirty.** The collision gate guarantees this — git's own write-time refusal is the second line of defense. Files the upgrade does *not* touch (other agents' `workdir/*`, unrelated scratch state) never block.\n- **Conflict markers always defer.** A repo with unresolved conflict markers is structurally broken regardless of upgrade scope; the gate refuses uniformly.\n- **Version stamps are atomic.** Either all migrations for the range succeed and the version is stamped, or no version is stamped — the next boot or `/lr:update` run can retry.\n- **No commits.** The user reviews and commits the upgraded files themselves.\n\n## Relationship to `/lr:update`\n\n`/lr:update` is the user-triggered, manual entry point — it processes all repos in the workspace and supports `--dry-run`. The boot-time check described here is a per-repo automatic reconciliation that runs only for the booting agent's repo, with no dry-run and no user prompt. Both share the same migration/release-notes data and the same version-stamping logic.\n\n**Asymmetry note:** the **write-aware collision gate (Step 1) lives only on the boot-time path.** `/lr:update` is user-triggered — the user has direct visibility of the workspace state, can `git diff` before running, and accepts that running `/lr:update` against dirty files may overwrite them. The boot-time path is automatic and unattended, so it carries the gate. `/lr:update` writes through dirty files unconditionally; the user is expected to commit/stash first. (Bringing the gate to `/lr:update` is a possible future enhancement.)\n\n## For Framework Authors\n\nWhen writing a new `migrations/<N>.md`, **declare its write-set** under a `## Write Paths` section (see `conventions.md` § Migration Write Paths). Without it, the boot-time gate falls back to the conservative blanket-dirty rule for the version range that includes your migration — meaning users with any unrelated dirty file will be blocked from auto-upgrading through your version. The declaration is mechanical (it's the same paths your migration's \"Steps\" section already touches) and the cost of omitting it is real friction.\n\nWhen changes touch plugin-cached state (skills, slash commands, scripts, SKILL.md-referenced docs), include the **Clear Plugin Cache** footer per `docs/conventions.md` § Migration / Release-Note Authoring. The cache-stale failure mode is invisible to the user until they invoke a missing skill — the footer makes the fix discoverable from the doc the user is already reading. See also `docs/doctor-stale-plugin-cache.md` for the underlying ailment.\n# Engine Profile — Claude Code (reference)\n\nSelected by boot (`agent-boot.md` Step 0) when `<framework-root>` lives under a Claude plugin\ndir (`~/.claude/plugins/…`) or the session was started with `--plugin-dir`. This is the\n**reference profile**: every other engine profile fills the same five bindings; only the values\ndiffer.\n\n## Binding values\n\n| Binding | Value on Claude Code |\n|---|---|\n| **framework-root** | Self-locate (Step 0). `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` also expands to it and may be used as a literal-path fallback. |\n| **invocation-syntax** | Skills are user-invoked as slash commands `/lr:<skill>`; the engine expands them. Canonical skill folders live under `skills/<skill>/`. Per-agent boot shortcuts `/lr-<agent>-agent`. |\n| **subagent-spawn** | The `Agent` tool. Fan-out = **N parallel `Agent` calls in a single message**. Sub-agent types: `general-purpose` (write), `Explore` (read-only, but excerpt-based — it locates material rather than reviewing it in depth), and `fork` (**inherits the caller's full conversation context**). Each subagent boots as its target agent and reads the procedure doc itself. Two traps: (1) `fork` is unsuitable wherever a subagent must be *independent* of the caller's reasoning — it carries the caller's context by design; (2) passing a **`name`** makes the call an Agent-Teams teammate, and **a teammate does not auto-return its final report to the caller** — only an unnamed call does. Spawn unnamed when you need the result back, or instruct a named teammate to `SendMessage` its report before going idle. |\n| **memory-file** | `CLAUDE.md`. |\n| **runtime-bounding** | The Bash-tool `timeout` parameter bounds a command's runtime. |\n\n## Capability gates\n\n- **teammate-detection** — supported. `lr-core preflight` walks up to six process ancestors,\n  reading each one's args with a single-field `ps -o args= -p <pid>` call and matching `--agent-id`\n  on a flag boundary (so `--agent-idle-timeout` does not false-positive); the engine is often the\n  grandparent rather than the immediate parent. Agent-Teams / `spawn-teammate` features are\n  available.\n\n## Registered shortcut bootstrap\n\nWhen `/lr:register-agent` or `/lr:register-repo` emits a Claude per-agent shortcut, use this\nexact body after substituting the agent values:\n\n```markdown\nRead the `SKILL.md` for the installed `/lr:boot` skill available in this session. Follow its\nself-location instruction to resolve `<framework-root>`, then read its `docs/agent-boot.md` and\nboot as agent `<agent-name>` from `<agent-dir>`.\n```\n\nDo not substitute `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`, an absolute plugin path, or a workspace checkout into\nthe shortcut. If the active boot skill is unavailable, follow this profile's normal fallback at the\npoint of use; generated shortcuts do not implement a second resolver.\n\n## Notes\n\n- This profile documents the historical default. The framework was authored on Claude Code, so\n  the shared procedure docs describe the Claude mechanism directly; other profiles state where\n  they diverge and win on conflict (`agent-boot.md` Step 0).\n# Engine Profile — Cursor Agent CLI\n\nSelected by boot (`agent-boot.md` Step 0) when the parent process arguments contain\n`cursor-agent`, or when `<framework-root>` lives under a Cursor-managed directory. Fills the same\nfive bindings as `claude.md` (the reference profile). **Where a value here conflicts with a shared\nprocedure doc, this profile wins for that step.**\n\nEmpirical basis: local Cursor Agent CLI trials on `cursor-agent 2026.07.01-41b2de7`, 2026-07-05,\nusing `--plugin-dir` against a local framework copy. Verified facts so far:\n\n- `--plugin-dir` works for local plugin loading.\n- User-facing slash invocation works (`/lr-boot lore-architect`). Skill folders live under\n  `.cursor-skills/lr-<skill>/` because Cursor shows the folder name in the picker — unlike Claude\n  Code, which prepends the plugin name as `/lr:<skill>`. The `.cursor-plugin/plugin.json` manifest\n  points skill discovery at that tree only.\n- `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` is empty in this engine path.\n- `ps -o args= -p $PPID` is permitted and exposes `cursor-agent` in the parent args.\n- Boot and recall work on real Cursor, but the framework otherwise falls back to the Claude\n  profile until this engine profile is present.\n\n## Binding values\n\n| Binding | Value on Cursor |\n|---|---|\n| **framework-root** | `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` is empty here — never rely on it. Use the absolute path self-located in Step 0. Reuse that resolved path everywhere `<framework-root>` appears. |\n| **invocation-syntax** | User-invoked lore skills work as slash commands (`/lr-<skill>`, e.g. `/lr-boot`, `/lr-recall`; per-agent shortcuts remain `/lr-<agent>-agent`) when the framework is loaded through `--plugin-dir` or installed as a Cursor plugin. The `.cursor-plugin/plugin.json` manifest sets `\"skills\": \".cursor-skills/\"` so only the `lr-`-prefixed wrappers load — canonical `skills/<skill>/` remains for Claude Code, while the hidden wrapper tree stays out of Codex's plugin skill crawl. Registered per-agent shortcuts are workspace-local skills under `.cursor/skills/`, generated by `/lr-register-agent` or `/lr-register-repo`, and path-scoped to the matching repo. When the plugin is not loaded, read `.cursor-skills/lr-<skill>/SKILL.md` (see § Mid-session fallback) — do not guess `docs/<skill>.md` paths. When loaded, invoke the skill by name or follow the same wrapper path for file-driven execution. |\n| **subagent-spawn** | **Conservative default, with one exception.** For procedures that describe Claude `Agent` fan-out, execute the work **host-side, serially, one target agent at a time** — read-only search work uses direct Read/Grep/Glob on each target lore directory; write work (merge, version reconcile, conflict resolution) runs inline in the host, scoped to one agent/repo at a time. This serial path is the validated one; keep using it. **Exception — procedures where subagent independence is the semantics, not an optimization** (currently only `trilens-loop.md`): use Cursor's native **`Task`** tool, since serial host-side execution would not be a slower version of the procedure but a different and invalid one. See § Native subagents below for what is documented versus validated. |\n| **memory-file** | `AGENTS.md`. |\n| **runtime-bounding** | No verified Claude-style Bash-tool `timeout` parameter is assumed here. Rely on Cursor's own job/approval controls plus the fast-fail git env vars in the shared docs. Ignore prose that specifically says to set a Bash-tool timeout parameter. |\n\n## Capability gates\n\n- **engine-detection** — `ps -o args= -p $PPID` works here and exposes the parent `cursor-agent`\n  command line. Use it as the strongest runtime signal for Cursor CLI sessions.\n- **teammate-detection** — do not run teammate-detection on Cursor boots. The `ps` probe itself\n  works, but Claude Agent-Teams semantics do not apply here, so Cursor should always treat the\n  session as a normal host session for lore purposes. `spawn-teammate` remains Claude-only.\n- **git / network approvals** — Cursor CLI exposes `--force`, `--trust`, and sandbox controls at\n  launch. In ordinary sessions, auto-pull / push may still be approval-gated or denied. Lore\n  procedures must degrade cleanly on denial rather than treating it as a framework failure.\n- **parallel worktrees** — Cursor has its own worktree support (`--worktree`, and broader\n  parallel-agent features in the product), but the framework has **not yet validated** how that\n  interacts with Lore's workspace invariant. Until proven, keep Lore's multi-agent file work\n  serial and host-driven on Cursor.\n- **native subagents** — documented by Cursor and usable, but **not yet validated end-to-end\n  against Lore's procedures**. See § Native subagents.\n\n## Native subagents\n\nCursor ships subagents; the framework's serial default above predates that and is retained because\nit is the validated path, not because subagents are absent.\n\nConfirmed against Cursor's official docs and changelog, 2026-07-25:\n\n- Subagents shipped in **Cursor 2.4** (2026-01-22), available in *\"the editor, CLI, and Cloud\n  Agents.\"*\n- The agent dispatches them with a **`Task` tool**. Several can run in parallel.\n- **Cursor 2.5** added async subagents and the nesting rule: a subagent may spawn its own subagents,\n  but one launched by a subagent cannot spawn further — i.e. one extra level below the top agent.\n- Definitions are markdown files with YAML frontmatter — `name`, `description`, `model`, `readonly`,\n  `is_background` — under `.cursor/agents/` (project) or `~/.cursor/agents/` (user). Cursor also\n  reads `.claude/agents/` and `.codex/agents/`, at both project and user level.\n- `cursor-agent --help` shows no spawn flag (checked against `2026.07.20-8cc9c0b`), which is\n  expected: `Task` is a model-facing in-session tool, exactly like Codex's `spawn_agent`.\n\nCommunity-sourced, **not** confirmed against an official Cursor changelog:\n\n- CLI support was reportedly broken for a period — the `Task` tool was not injected into the CLI\n  agent's toolset, so subagents ran serially inside one context — and reportedly fixed for standard\n  accounts in Q1 2026. Forum threads spanning roughly Feb–Apr 2026 describe both the bug and the\n  fix; no official changelog entry for it could be located. Treat the bug as real and the fix date as\n  unverified. The practical consequence either way: on an old CLI build `Task` may be absent, so a\n  procedure that needs it should check rather than assume.\n\nEither way the conservative binding in this profile was written 2026-07-05, well after the reported\nfix — it was simply never rechecked.\n\n**Unvalidated and load-bearing:** whether `Task` accepts a **free-text brief** (like Claude's\n`Agent`) or only dispatches a **pre-defined agent file by name**. A procedure that composes its\nbriefs per invocation cannot pre-declare them. Until this is measured, a procedure using `Task` on\nCursor must handle the name-only case — write throwaway definitions under `.cursor/agents/`\n(`readonly: true`), dispatch, then delete them — and say in its output that it did so.\n\nDo not extend `Task` use to the serial-default procedures (recall, consult, attach, merge, conflict\nresolution) on the strength of this section alone. Those have a passing serial path; changing them\nneeds its own validation run.\n\n## Detecting \"plugin not loaded\"\n\n**Do not use `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`** — it is always empty on Cursor, even when the plugin loaded\ncorrectly.\n\nUse: an expected plugin skill (e.g. `/lr-boot`) **not available** in the session skill list. If\nskills are listed but show stale content after an upgrade, see `doctor-stale-plugin-cache.md`\n(Claude cache) or the refresh contract below (Cursor).\n\n## Registered shortcut bootstrap\n\nWhen `/lr-register-agent` or `/lr-register-repo` emits a Cursor per-agent shortcut, use this\nexact body after substituting the agent values:\n\n```markdown\nRead the `SKILL.md` for the installed `/lr-boot` skill available in this session. Follow its\nself-location instruction to resolve `<framework-root>`, then read its `docs/agent-boot.md` and\nboot as agent `<agent-name>` from `<agent-dir>`.\n```\n\nDo not substitute an absolute plugin path, a workspace checkout, or a cache-discovery heuristic.\nIf the active boot skill is unavailable, use the Mid-session fallback below; emitted shortcuts do\nnot contain that fallback.\n\n## Mid-session fallback (canonical)\n\nWhen plugin skills are unavailable and the user provides a checkout path:\n\n1. Treat that path as `<framework-root>` (must contain `VERSION`).\n2. Read `docs/engines/cursor.md` (this file) for engine bindings.\n3. For a requested operation `/lr-<skill>`: read `.cursor-skills/lr-<skill>/SKILL.md` and follow\n   it (it delegates to the correct `docs/<procedure>.md`).\n\n**Name mapping is not mechanical** — always use the wrapper SKILL, not `docs/<skill>.md` guesswork.\nExamples: `lr-boot` → `agent-boot.md`; `lr-merge` → `process-merge.md`; `lr-register-repo` →\n`register-repo.md`.\n\nThe user must communicate the checkout path (paste in chat, `AGENTS.md`, or workspace layout). This\npath works **before** boot — Step 0 engine profile is optional when executing file-driven.\n\n## Plugin load, refresh, and load surfaces (operational)\n\n### Refresh contract\n"
  }
]

### 73. User — 2026-07-28T12:03:49.009Z

Can you explain this?
rrected the manual-fallback version-skew wording in commit 6c0cd60 on codex/shortcut-bootstrap

### 74. Assistant — 2026-07-28T12:03:54.845Z

It’s a documentation-only correction inside `scripts/lr-core`; it does not change runtime behavior.

When a lore repo version differs from the installed framework version, `lr-core` deliberately returns the result in structured `data.version`. Then `agent-boot.md` reads `docs/version-check.md` to give the correct engine-specific remedy—for example, Codex plugin refresh commands.

The script’s manual-fallback docstring incorrectly said version skew was itself emitted as a warning. An agent following the fallback could therefore print a generic warning and skip the proper remedy. Commit `6c0cd60` changes that wording to match the actual behavior: version skew is structured data, and the caller renders the user-facing guidance.

You need this to know which trees and which files to name for reviewers — not to\n   hand them a diff. **If the scope comes out empty, stop and say so**; don't spawn reviewers over\n   nothing.\n2. **Lenses.** A lens is a *way of looking* — one perspective from which this session's changes\n   deserve to be examined. Pick the three that matter most for *these* changes, and make them\n   genuinely different from each other: not places to look, not overlapping targets. Three\n   different lenses over the same whole scope beats three reviewers splitting it between them,\n   which just buys you the same thinking three times.\n\n   **Choosing them is your job, and yours alone.** Nobody else in the loop knows what you just did;\n   the reviewers arrive cold by design. So reason from the change itself — what kind of work was\n   this, and what are the three ways it is most likely to be wrong? A schema migration, a rewritten\n   procedure doc, and a new CLI command each deserve a different trio. Reaching for the same three\n   lenses every time defeats the point as surely as picking three lenses that say the same thing.\n3. **Spawn.** Spawn **subagents** — one cold, read-only subagent per lens, using whatever subagent\n   mechanism your engine gives you. You don't need to look anything up first. What you do need is\n   real independence: it is the deliverable here, not a speed-up, so there is **no host-side\n   fallback**. If you cannot spawn independent subagents at all, stop and say so — running the\n   lenses yourself, in sequence, is a different procedure wearing this one's name. Each reviewer\n   returns findings tagged `BLOCKER` / `HIGH` / `MEDIUM` / `LOW`, and one overall verdict: `SHIP`,\n   `SHIP-WITH-FIXES`, or `BLOCK`. See the exchange contract below for what may cross the wire.\n\n   Run reviewers on a **regular model tier, not a flagship one** — sonnet on Claude Code,\n   composer-2.5 on Cursor, gpt-5.4 on Codex. Those are illustrations, not a lookup table: pick\n   whichever current model is your engine's regular tier. What buys quality here is more rounds and\n   genuinely independent lenses, not a more expensive reviewer. Escalate a single lens only when\n   that lens has demonstrably failed at the regular tier.\n4. **Triage — yours alone.** Check every `BLOCKER` and `HIGH` against the file it points at (lower\n   severities at your discretion), decide whether it is real and worth addressing, apply what is,\n   decline the rest with a one-line reason. Each finding ends in exactly one of three states:\n   `APPLIED`, `DECLINED`, or `ACCEPTED` — the last for a finding you agree with but are not acting\n   on *here* (out of scope, or fixes were turned off), which is a legitimate outcome and must not be\n   filed as either of the other two.\n5. **Repeat, if the changes are considerable.** Re-resolve the scope first — your own fixes just\n   changed it. Fresh reviewers each round, each told how the previous round disposed of every\n   finding, accepted ones included, so settled findings don't resurface. Stop as soon as a round\n   comes back with nothing worth fixing — if that is round one, you are done, that is a good\n   outcome and not a reason to keep looking. **Never more than three rounds.**\n6. **Report, briefly.** Close with a few lines, not an essay: rounds run, the three lenses, each\n   finding as one line with its state, and anything you overrode.\n\n## Stopping rules\n\n- A round where a lens did not actually report is **not** a clean round for that lens — retry the\n  missing ones once, and that retry does not count against the three-round ceiling. If they come\n  back silent again, say which lenses never reported and stop; never bank a silent round as clean.\n- Never call it done while a reviewer's latest verdict is `BLOCK`, without saying out loud that you\n  are overriding it.\n\n## Exchange contract\n\nKeep both sides lean. This is how the loop is meant to run at any scale, not a fallback for tight\nbudgets.\n\n- **Host → reviewer:** which repos and working trees to look at, the plain list of files that\n  changed, a sentence or two on what you were trying to do, and which lens it owns. The file list\n  and the task give the reviewer its bearings — what the change is *for* — without telling it what\n  to think. **Don't hand it the diff or the contents of anything**; it reads the files itself. The\n  fresh look is most of what you are paying for, so stop at orientation: no walkthrough of your\n  reasoning, no account of what you believe you got right.\n- **Reviewer → host:** the findings and the verdict, nothing else. Each finding is a pointer into\n  code or docs that already exists — path, line, and the signature or heading sitting there — plus\n  roughly a paragraph on what is wrong at that spot, plus its severity.\n- **Never crosses the wire:** pasted files, quoted excerpts, or a blow-by-blow account of what you\n  changed and why it is right. Orientation is a sentence or two; past that you are reviewing your\n  own work on the reviewer's behalf, which is the one thing you spawned it to avoid.\n\n## Overrides\n\nFree text overrides any of the above — fewer rounds, narrower scope, even a self-review. Just\ndisclose whatever you turned off. The three-round ceiling is the one thing it does not lift.\n"

    "text": "# Trilens Loop\n\n`/lr:trilens-loop` reviews the session's changes from **three** independent, cold-context\nperspectives — three by default because differing perspectives are the whole idea, and never\nsubagents that inherit this conversation, since independence from the author is the entire point.\n\n## The loop\n\n1. **Scope.** Resolve it yourself: the files you changed, union with `git status`/`diff` in every\n   repo involved. You need this to know which trees and which files to name for reviewers — not to\n   hand them a diff. **If the scope comes out empty, stop and say so**; don't spawn reviewers over\n   nothing.\n2. **Lenses.** A lens is a *way of looking* — one perspective from which this session's changes\n   deserve to be examined. Pick the three that matter most for *these* changes, and make them\n   genuinely different from each other: not places to look, not overlapping targets. Three\n   different lenses over the same whole scope beats three reviewers splitting it between them,\n   which just buys you the same thinking three times.\n\n   **Choosing them is your job, and yours alone.** Nobody else in the loop knows what you just did;\n   the reviewers arrive cold by design. So reason from the change itself — what kind of work was\n   this, and what are the three ways it is most likely to be wrong? A schema migration, a rewritten\n   procedure doc, and a new CLI command each deserve a different trio. Reaching for the same three\n   lenses every time defeats the point as surely as picking three lenses that say the same thing.\n3. **Spawn.** Spawn **subagents** — one cold, read-only subagent per lens, using whatever subagent\n   mechanism your engine gives you. You don't need to look anything up first. What you do need is\n   real independence: it is the deliverable here, not a speed-up, so there is **no host-side\n   fallback**. If you cannot spawn independent subagents at all, stop and say so — running the\n   lenses yourself, in sequence, is a different procedure wearing this one's name. Each reviewer\n   returns findings tagged `BLOCKER` / `HIGH` / `MEDIUM` / `LOW`, and one overall verdict: `SHIP`,\n   `SHIP-WITH-FIXES`, or `BLOCK`. See the exchange contract below for what may cross the wire.\n\n   Run reviewers on a **regular model tier, not a flagship one** — sonnet on Claude Code,\n   composer-2.5 on Cursor, gpt-5.4 on Codex. Those are illustrations, not a lookup table: pick\n   whichever current model is your engine's regular tier. What buys quality here is more rounds and\n   genuinely independent lenses, not a more expensive reviewer. Escalate a single lens only when\n   that lens has demonstrably failed at the regular tier.\n4. **Triage — yours alone.** Check every `BLOCKER` and `HIGH` against the file it points at (lower\n   severities at your discretion), decide whether it is real and worth addressing, apply what is,\n   decline the rest with a one-line reason. Each finding ends in exactly one of three states:\n   `APPLIED`, `DECLINED`, or `ACCEPTED` — the last for a finding you agree with but are not acting\n   on *here* (out of scope, or fixes were turned off), which is a legitimate outcome and must not be\n   filed as either of the other two.\n5. **Repeat, if the changes are considerable.** Re-resolve the scope first — your own fixes just\n   changed it. Fresh reviewers each round, each told how the previous round disposed of every\n   finding, accepted ones included, so settled findings don't resurface. Stop as soon as a round\n   comes back with nothing worth fixing — if that is round one, you are done, that is a good\n   outcome and not a reason to keep looking. **Never more than three rounds.**\n6. **Report, briefly.** Close with a few lines, not an essay: rounds run, the three lenses, each\n   finding as one line with its state, and anything you overrode.\n\n## Stopping rules\n\n- A round where a lens did not actually report is **not** a clean round for that lens — retry the\n  missing ones once, and that retry does not count against the three-round ceiling. If they come\n  back silent again, say which lenses never reported and stop; never bank a silent round as clean.\n- Never call it done while a reviewer's latest verdict is `BLOCK`, without saying out loud that you\n  are overriding it.\n\n## Exchange contract\n\nKeep both sides lean. This is how the loop is meant to run at any scale, not a fallback for tight\nbudgets.\n\n- **Host → reviewer:** which repos and working trees to look at, the plain list of files that\n  changed, a sentence or two on what you were trying to do, and which lens it owns. The file list\n  and the task give the reviewer its bearings — what the change is *for* — without telling it what\n  to think. **Don't hand it the diff or the contents of anything**; it reads the files itself. The\n  fresh look is most of what you are paying for, so stop at orientation: no walkthrough of your\n  reasoning, no account of what you believe you got right.\n- **Reviewer → host:** the findings and the verdict, nothing else. Each finding is a pointer into\n  code or docs that already exists — path, line, and the signature or heading sitting there — plus\n  roughly a paragraph on what is wrong at that spot, plus its severity.\n- **Never crosses the wire:** pasted files, quoted excerpts, or a blow-by-blow account of what you\n  changed and why it is right. Orientation is a sentence or two; past that you are reviewing your\n  own work on the reviewer's behalf, which is the one thing you spawned it to avoid.\n\n## Overrides\n\nFree text overrides any of the above — fewer rounds, narrower scope, even a self-review. Just\ndisclose whatever you turned off. The three-round ceiling is the one thing it does not lift.\n"
  }
]

If the returns aren't retained, phase 3 loses the list of lore changes per guest.\n\n## Phase 3 — Summarize\n\nRead `<framework-root>/docs/summarize.md` and follow it. Writes the canonical summary into the host agent's `sessions/YYYY/MM/` directory, the host full Markdown session log into `archive/YYYY/MM/`, and — for every attached guest that had lore updates in phase 2 — a short guest summary into the guest's `sessions/YYYY/MM/`. All summaries and the full log share the session UUID. Summarize is additive — its failure does not roll back reflect or merge.\n\nPhase 3 includes summarize Step 1.5: resolving the native session log and writing the host full Markdown archive plus `usage`/`archive` frontmatter. This is a **required attempt before phase 4**, not an optional enhancement. Skip it only when Step 1.5's command-level rules say to skip (no resolved log path, non-zero stats/archive command, privacy concern, or another explicit error). Before committing, verify the host summary either contains both `usage:` and `archive:` and the archive Markdown file exists with matching frontmatter, or that you have reported the exact Step 1.5 reason for omitting them.\n\n## Phase 4 — Commit and Push\n\nCollect every repo touched by phases 1–3 — each active agent's repo (for its own lore updates), plus the host's repo (for the canonical summary and archive), plus any guest repo that received a short guest summary from phase 3. When host and guests share a repo, their changes go into a single commit for that repo. Then, for each such repo:\n\n1. `git -C <repo> add agents/` — scoped to the agent tree so incidental untracked files elsewhere are not swept in.\n2. `git -C <repo> commit -m \"Finalize session <short-uuid>\"`.\n3. `git -C <repo> push`.\n\nFor each repo, print a one-line confirmation (e.g., `✓ <repo>: committed <sha>, pushed to <branch>`). No approval prompt — phase 4 runs end-to-end without user interaction.\n\n### Failure handling\n\n- **Summarize failed** — commit the reflect+merge output alone. Merge output is valuable on its own; don't hold it hostage to the summary.\n- **Any merge subagent failed** — do not commit in that repo. Report the failure and let the user resolve before retrying finalize.\n- **Push rejected due to conflicts in an agent subtree** — this happens when another user (or parallel session) finalized the same agent concurrently. Trigger the conflict-resolution procedure: read `<framework-root>/docs/resolve-conflicts.md` and follow it. One subagent is spawned per conflicted agent; each boots as its agent, reconciles its own subtree, and retries push up to 3 times against concurrent races.\n- **Push fails for other reasons** (auth, remote unreachable, conflicts outside agent subtrees) — the commit is already made locally. Report the failure and let the user resolve manually.\n\n### Cross-repo guests\n\nIf a guest is attached from a different lore agent repo, phase 2 touches that guest's repo and phase 4 commits there too. The guest also receives a short guest summary in its own repo (see `summarize.md`). Each repo gets its own commit + push.\n\n### Partial push failure across repos\n\nPush order across repos is undefined. If one repo's push succeeds and another's fails (network, auth, conflicts), the public record is momentarily asymmetric — e.g., the host summary is visible while a guest summary isn't yet. The local commits are already made; the user can retry the failed repo's push manually. Do not roll back the successful repos to \"restore symmetry\" — that would discard work.\n\n## Invariants\n\n- **One commit per touched repo.** Reflect, merge, and summarize output land in a single commit per repo — not split across phases.\n- **Fully automated.** Finalize runs end-to-end without approval prompts. Phase 3's summary display in `summarize.md` step 12 is the user's view of what was recorded; git history is the post-hoc review channel.\n- **Push is part of finalize.** Standalone reflect/merge/summarize do not push; only `/lr:finalize` does.\n- **No empty commits.** If nothing was produced by phases 1–3 in a given repo, skip committing in that repo.\n\n## When to use\n\nRun at the end of a working session. Do not run finalize for a consultation (`/lr:consult` is already ephemeral) or for a session that produced no lore changes and no summary-worthy narrative.\n"
